Read-only substantive review of the P16 governing proposal. Codex remains executor. Do not edit, launch agents, run commands, access network or change state. The background master is stopped. Product runtime implementation is disabled.

The user wants execution here, with one meaningful integrated review, not more controller work. Judge the actual specification and its interactions. Check inconsistent identities/replay/commit claims, status loss, unknown-to-false coercion, scope/admission, hidden defaults, invalid financial or probability arithmetic, omitted reference bindings, unsupported claims and whether a P16 architecture can govern later detailed methods. Distinguish a material P16 architecture defect from explicitly retained P17 method/source obligations and P18/P19 manuscript/readiness work. Do not demand already deferred product runtime or professional review. Missing real E05 conflict and a complete real package remain genuine proposal blockers, not completed evidence.

Return substantive findings with file/section, counterexample, smallest repair and whether they block P16 acceptance. Avoid procedural review chains or file-count criteria. State inspected scope and uninspected limitations. End with VERDICT: AGREE or VERDICT: REVISE.

Executed checks: python3 platform-contract-calculation-v1.py returned 169/169 exact finite checks; accounting-complete-exchanges-diagnostic-v1.py returned 2580/2580 checks including 27 new exchanges, 17 retained controls and V01–V16; unchanged check_accounting_proposal.py --phase P16 --check returned 797/797 structural checks before final canonicalization wording refinement; 932 protected originals unchanged. Those counts are document/structural evidence, not execution of a new compiler, solver or store. Current plan: docs/plans/accounting-p16-direct-execution-20261006.md. Current result and progress: data/accounting-master/deliverables/P16/. Reports: data/accounting-master/direct-p16-20261006/.

Review focus: existing evidence/time/history/uncertainty semantics, chaining/admission and their compatibility with new interfaces/profile. Independent uncertainty acceptance was not yet recorded.

FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/evidence-contract-v3.md
SHA256: 688dab1b7f194eb89abf789c70326463892f41d7893662a8b720300cb393801f
1: # Evidence contract v3 — proposed semantics
2: 
3: This contract addresses MC01–MC04 and the admission boundary of MC27
4: (G2/G11/G17). It specifies evidence preparation for the whole product, including
5: later group, bank, insurance and cross-standard work. It does not select an
6: initial product profile. P15-E01–E03 receive concrete proposed answers; E05
7: still needs a real conflicting-disclosure walkthrough. Examples are in
8: `evidence-fixtures-v3.json`. Synthetic text is identified there and supplies
9: no accounting authority.
10: 
11: ## E1. Input and output records
12: 
13: An input `SourceVersion` contains immutable original bytes, their SHA-256,
14: media type, source URL or original local origin, acquisition event, issuer,
15: document identity/version, and publication evidence. An extracted text file is
16: a separate derivative with its own hash, extraction method/version and link to
17: the original. Reacquiring different bytes creates a new version even if the URL
18: is unchanged. A URL, matching title or current download does not establish the
19: bytes available at an earlier date. The time contract determines that claim.
20: 
21: A `UnitOccurrence` identifies one source version and a locator: PDF physical
22: page plus printed page and bounding region where available; HTML element or
23: XBRL fact/context identifiers; text line/character span in a pinned extraction.
24: Locators retain table headings, footnotes and continuation links needed for
25: meaning. A text locator alone does not establish faithful PDF extraction.
26: Units identify parent section, annual/quarterly/semiannual package root, and
27: disposition. An occurrence is never deleted because another occurrence has the
28: same text. Byte-identical span content may share a content ID; semantic
29: equivalence additionally requires compatible context and an explicit review.
30: 
31: A `ReportedAssertion` links every supporting occurrence and preserves literal
32: text/value. An `ExtractionReading` identifies a candidate reading and its
33: review events. An `Interpretation` identifies the exact extraction versions,
34: financial scope, dependencies, modality and proposed meaning. A `Derivation`
35: records input versions, expression, result context and assumptions. An
36: `Assumption` has an author, rationale, scope and alternatives and cannot be
37: labelled issuer evidence. A `Conflict` links incompatible candidates and their
38: affected queries; conflicting faithful readings both survive.
39: 
40: Preparation returns (1) immutable source and reading records, (2) a package
41: coverage report with three separate populations, (3) proposed, admitted and
42: unresolved predicates, (4) query-specific evidence exposure, and (5) typed
43: issues containing record IDs, locators, dependencies and repair actions.
44: Every output identifies the evidence view and review snapshot. Neither an
45: extraction confidence score nor review acceptance supplies a probability over
46: company histories.
47: 
48: ## E2. Package closure and three populations
49: 
50: Start with declared annual, quarterly and semiannual roots, dates, included
51: sections and expected statement/note indexes. Reconcile contents with actual
52: headings and tables, then follow references within the declared material scope.
53: A reference edge retains its source locator, target description, candidates,
54: resolution evidence and disposition. The permitted dispositions in these
55: fixtures are `resolved`, `duplicate_verified`, `cycle_closed`,
56: `missing_continuation`, `ambiguous_target`, `unavailable_target`, and
57: `resource_exhausted`. A verified cycle stops repeated traversal; its target must
58: exist and the closing edge remains visible. Similar names alone cannot resolve
59: an ambiguous target. A budget limit retains the unvisited frontier and the
60: limit reached, never an empty-success result.
61: 
62: The acquired-unit population counts retained occurrences and unique contents
63: separately. Its denominator is the declared inventory, with discovered missing
64: targets and unknown inventory extent reported alongside it. Parent and child
65: units cannot both contribute to a count of nonoverlapping leaf spans. A repeated
66: paragraph contributes two occurrence locators but one content body; this does
67: not merge assertions applying to different dates or entities.
68: 
69: The reviewed-assertion population counts identified assertion candidates at a
70: named layer, once per contextual assertion identity. It reports accepted,
71: rejected, pending and unresolved counts and aliases explicitly. It also retains
72: unread/unstructured source units, whose unknown assertion count cannot be
73: treated as zero. A denominator of six discovered candidates means six candidates
74: reviewed to some disposition, not six assertions in the whole real report.
75: 
76: The query-exposure population is the declared query set and each query's
77: transitive evidence dependencies. Each query is `supported`, `conditional`,
78: `unresolved`, or `unsupported` for its requested claim. `supported` means the
79: specified evidence-reading operation has met its admission rules, not that the
80: issuer's report is true or compliant. `conditional` requires explicit selected
81: assumptions; an unselected assumption leaves the query unresolved. Missing or
82: ambiguous references block only dependent claims, but uncertain relevance must
83: be reported as an unresolved dependency until reviewed. An unaffected disclosed
84: revenue lookup can succeed while debt-exposure reconstruction remains unresolved.
85: 
86: Package closure requires no unresolved material references, reconciled inventory
87: and a documented scope/materiality decision for exclusions. Semantic completion
88: additionally requires every in-scope unit to have its assertions and unresolved
89: meaning accounted for. Three root files, all retained known units, or a high
90: review count cannot establish either condition. The synthetic fixture has seven
91: occurrences, five unique contents, six contextual assertion candidates, seven
92: reference edges and five queries; its four unresolved edges veto package closure.
93: 
94: ## E3. Numeric context and faithful display
95: 
96: A numeric reading includes legal entity and perimeter/version, concept and
97: mapping version, dimensions, instant or half-open reporting interval, fiscal
98: calendar/version, currency, unit, scale, sign convention, measurement basis,
99: reported lexeme, display decimals, and separately the rounding convention and
100: tie rule with their source or assumption references. Unknown fields are tagged
101: unknown, not supplied by a global currency/calendar default. Zero, missing,
102: not-applicable and a printed dash are distinct; a dash becomes zero only under
103: a supported interpretation.
104: 
105: For a reported lexeme 364,980 with USD and a million scale, the exact *scaled
106: displayed value* is 364,980,000,000 USD. That is not a claim that the issuer's
107: underlying unrounded amount equals it. Rendering must reproduce the lexeme,
108: scale label, sign convention and precision, including parentheses, without
109: round-tripping through binary floating point. Derived displays retain their
110: expression and do not masquerade as original disclosure.
111: 
112: For arithmetic, currencies, dimensions, bases, perimeters and concepts must
113: match or have an admitted explicit conversion/mapping. Flows require compatible
114: nonoverlapping intervals for addition; stocks are not summed over dates.
115: YTD subtraction follows T5 in the time contract. Mismatches return
116: `INCOMPATIBLE_CONTEXT`; unknown compatibility returns `UNRESOLVED_CONTEXT`.
117: Permissive archival display is possible without authorising arithmetic.
118: 
119: If source evidence establishes rounding to nearest unit u, a displayed value d
120: has interior compatibility d-u/2 < x < d+u/2. Endpoint membership follows the
121: specified tie rule. For ties-to-even, displayed integer 120 admits both half-unit
122: endpoints; 121 admits neither. With nearest established but tie rule unknown,
123: the closed interval is only an outer enclosure: both endpoint memberships are
124: unresolved. A query requiring exact endpoint feasibility must return unresolved.
125: If even the nearest convention is unknown, displayed precision alone supplies
126: no such interval. A user may explicitly choose assumption `nearest-model-v1`;
127: its interval and every dependent result then remain conditional. None of these
128: intervals supplies a distribution. P02's closed half-unit model is evidence of
129: a prior compatibility assumption, not issuer confirmation of that convention.
130: 
131: ## E4. Narrative interpretation and admission
132: 
133: A narrative interpretation binds subject/entity, contract or instrument set,
134: valid dates, quantifier, modality (`reported_fact`, `permission`,
135: `conditional_obligation`, `policy`), predicate, exceptions, dependent facts and
136: affected quantities. Missing scope is unresolved, not universal. Literal wording
137: is retained even when no executable predicate is available.
138: 
139: “May repurchase up to 50” permits an action in the specified period and currency;
140: it imposes neither a purchase nor a payment. A selected scenario may add an
141: exercise decision and resulting cash flow, separately attributed as an
142: assumption. “Pays if borrower defaults” preserves the condition; with default
143: unknown it cannot become an unconditional payable. “Substantially all” supplies
144: no 95% threshold. A proposed threshold must be a named modelling assumption
145: and cannot replace the retained qualitative claim.
146: 
147: The source-backed Apple example uses Note 9 of the 2024 10-K (physical page 46, printed page 43),
148: the fixed-rate debt table and the adjacent swap paragraph. The coupon
149: classification applies to notes. The paragraph says certain USD notes are
150: effectively converted to floating rates by swaps; it does not identify each
151: instrument allocation here. Preserve a combined exposure query as unresolved
152: pending allocation and terms. Reject a translation stating all combined
153: note-and-swap cash flows are fixed. The adjacent Note 4 wording “may use” is a
154: policy/permission description, not evidence that every instrument is hedged.
155: These passages are compatible qualifications, not a genuine source conflict.
156: 
157: Review events name target ID/version, layer, action, reviewer/time and reason.
158: At each layer a candidate progresses `proposed -> accepted | rejected |
159: unresolved`. A new alternative starts at proposed. A correction creates a new
160: candidate and an explicit supersession link; it does not overwrite source bytes
161: or retroactively change old review snapshots. Rejected/unresolved candidates
162: can be reconsidered only by a new recorded decision. Extraction acceptance
163: means faithful reading; interpretation acceptance means accepted scoped meaning;
164: accounting admission additionally requires applicable treatment authority,
165: context and rule/version. No transition automatically accepts a higher layer.
166: 
167: Read-only disclosed-value queries can use accepted extraction and context.
168: Constraint queries require an accepted interpretation, admitted translation,
169: resolved predicates or explicit conditional assumptions. Posting requests also
170: require accounting admission. Dependency corrections invalidate current use of
171: transitive descendants; old results remain retrievable under their old evidence
172: view with a current superseded warning. Unrelated descendants stay usable.
173: The storage transaction and identity mechanics remain a later P16 contract.
174: 
175: ## E5. Failure, alternatives and interaction obligations
176: 
177: Integrity mismatch or dangling required source IDs rejects the affected
178: record; missing meaning produces a retained unresolved record. A conflicting
179: accepted pair returns both alternatives plus affected-query status, never
180: last-write-wins. Unsupported translations return `UNSUPPORTED_PREDICATE` with
181: the original text. Resource limits return partial inventories and unfinished
182: frontiers; they cannot certify impossibility or complete coverage.
183: 
184: The main alternative is one aggregate completeness score. It is simpler to
185: display but hides missing contracts behind parsed pages; separate populations
186: are proposed because each answers a different question. Another alternative
187: lets accepted free text constrain a model implicitly. Explicit scoped admission
188: costs annotation work but prevents permissions, exceptions and qualitative
189: terms from acquiring invented mathematical meaning. Automatically inferring
190: nearest rounding is convenient but overstates source support; prohibiting all
191: conditional analysis is unnecessarily restrictive. Separate source facts and
192: named assumptions support both faithful reporting and useful conditional work.
193: 
194: Time supplies eligible source/review versions; history families receive admitted
195: constraints and unresolved predicates without manufactured weights; accounting
196: receives evidence plus authority requirements; language/IR must preserve every
197: scope and assumption; persistence must retain immutable versions and dependency
198: invalidation; interfaces must expose all three coverage populations and reasons.
199: Those counterpart contracts remain unfinished. Independent substantive review,
200: a real conflicting-disclosure case and later domain authority work are required
201: before closing E05 or the wider mission gaps.
202: 
203: ## Revision record
204: 
205: v2 retains the v1 contract semantics and points to the corrected fixture set.
206: E-display describes assets and now names its sign convention `positive_assets`;
207: the v1 revenue sign label was wrong for that context. The scaled displayed
208: amount remains 364,980,000,000 USD. No source statement was changed.
209: 
210: ## v3 source-locator correction
211: 
212: Original PDF inspection in e05-source-investigation-v1.md corrects the Note 9
213: locator. No substantive admission rule or expected output has changed; v3 fixtures
214: correct the same locator and remain the examples for E1–E5. E05 source evidence remains insufficient.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/time-contract-v2.md
SHA256: 3064ee8dde2c93543ffcaf7b1357d6ca1f6c8d50e61e2a2aea14b190b014cccd
1: # Time contract v2 — proposed semantics
2: 
3: This contract addresses MC05 with MC04/MC25/MC27 interactions (G13/G17/G18).
4: It supplies the proposed answer to P15-E04. Its fixtures are specification
5: examples, not a runtime implementation. The existing P06 chronology selector
6: is a counterexample where it substitutes availability for absent ingestion and
7: allows unverified historical bytes by default. Neither behaviour is adopted
8: for a claim requiring the missing evidence.
9: 
10: ## T1. Records and query dimensions
11: 
12: An assertion version identifies economic instant or event dates, reporting
13: interval and calendar, public availability evidence, ingestion event, review
14: events, source byte hash and evidence of byte vintage. These are different
15: clocks. A payment date does not establish recognition time; reporting period
16: end does not establish publication. A later acquisition may preserve bytes
17: known to have been public earlier without establishing system possession then.
18: 
19: Economic and reporting periods use ISO calendar dates and explicit calendar
20: versions; flow intervals are [start, end_exclusive). Timestamp comparisons use
21: timezone-aware instants converted to UTC. Preserve the original timezone,
22: precision and source representation. Missing timezone rejects an instant
23: comparison (`MISSING_TIMEZONE`); it never means the machine's local zone.
24: Absent ingestion and explicit-null ingestion are separate diagnostic states,
25: `INGESTION_ABSENT` and `INGESTION_UNKNOWN`, but neither establishes an instant.
26: 
27: A query specifies `admission_mode`, `selection`, economic/reporting target,
28: information cutoff when applicable, system-as-of when applicable, review
29: snapshot, requested layer, and calendar/perimeter/measurement basis. Selection
30: is `original`, `latest_available`, or `harmonised`. No selection overrides
31: admission. Omitted required cutoffs or a nonexistent review snapshot reject the
32: request, rather than silently choosing today's data. Outputs contain selected
33: IDs, known exclusions/reasons, unresolved candidates/dependencies, derivation
34: lineage, assumptions, and claim status. Historical simulation receives only
35: admitted evidence; diagnostic knowledge of later rejected IDs belongs in the
36: audit output and must not become scenario input.
37: 
38: ## T2. Three admission modes
39: 
40: `archival_exploration` allows inspection of retained bytes, including unknown
41: publication or ingestion. Such rows are labelled `historical_admissibility:
42: unestablished`. With a cutoff and unknown publication they may be displayed only
43: as unresolved archival candidates, not as cutoff-qualified selections. Without
44: a cutoff all retained rows may be selected under explicit lineage rules, but
45: the result remains archival; no historical knowledge claim follows. Source
46: integrity checks still apply. Exploration does not authorise accounting posting.
47: 
48: `investor_information` requires information cutoff K, supported public
49: availability at or before K, and evidence linking the exact selected source
50: bytes to that availability. Later ingestion is permitted. Unknown publication,
51: unverified historical bytes or date precision straddling K leaves a candidate
52: unresolved and excludes it from the admitted set. Known publication after K
53: is a definite exclusion. An accepted extraction performed later may read an
54: eligible old document using an explicitly chosen review snapshot. Any derived
55: interpretation that consumes later information is inadmissible for a historical
56: decision rule; its full information dependencies must pass K separately.
57: This view establishes public availability of evidence, not actual investor
58: awareness or a tradeable execution opportunity.
59: 
60: `system_as_of` requires S and an immutable receipt proving exact bytes were
61: ingested no later than S. Ingestion cannot be inferred from publication. The
62: requested accepted layer additionally needs review decisions visible by S;
63: later review cannot be projected back. Retained raw evidence may be returned
64: with its then-unreviewed status if the requested layer is raw. System possession
65: alone does not establish public availability. If a query also requests an
66: investor cutoff K, it must satisfy both filters, including public byte-vintage
67: evidence. Unknown publication alone need not block a *pure* system-possession
68: query. This separation permits later legitimate private/local records without
69: claiming that they were public.
70: 
71: A known later revision is excluded by its actual time; it does not suppress
72: an earlier eligible assertion. An unresolved potentially relevant version
73: keeps a result unresolved even if an older candidate is selected. Unaffected
74: keys retain supported results. `unresolved` is different from an empty set
75: proved to contain no eligible records.
76: 
77: ## T3. Date-only evidence and byte verification
78: 
79: A publication date D with named timezone Z denotes an interval from local
80: midnight D through (but excluding) the next local midnight. The physical
81: instant is unknown within it. If K is before the interval, exclude the record;
82: if K is inside, return `PUBLICATION_PRECISION_UNRESOLVED`; if K is at or after
83: the interval's exclusive upper boundary, admission is supported by that date
84: evidence. This is a conservative availability convention, not a claim that
85: publication occurred at midnight. No fixed 24-hour duration is assumed across
86: daylight-saving changes. Missing Z keeps the interval unresolved. Conflicting
87: publication sources remain alternatives until resolved; choose neither the
88: earliest nor latest silently.
89: 
90: An ingestion date without an instant similarly proves only bounded possession;
91: queries inside the date remain unresolved. Review date precision is treated
92: the same way. A receipt must bind timestamp evidence, source ID and byte hash;
93: `ingested_at` alone without a receipt is `INGESTION_RECEIPT_UNVERIFIED`.
94: Public byte-vintage evidence and system receipt evidence are different proofs.
95: Current byte integrity failure is a hard rejection (`SOURCE_HASH_MISMATCH`),
96: whereas missing historical linkage is unresolved. An issuer timestamp on a
97: current webpage cannot by itself prove unchanged historical bytes.
98: 
99: ## T4. Selection, supersession and review state
100: 
101: Supersession edges identify assertion IDs and the exact concept, period,
102: perimeter and dimensions replaced, plus supporting evidence. The graph must be
103: acyclic and targets must exist; a cycle or missing target rejects the affected
104: lineage. A document-wide replacement is not inferred from one corrected fact.
105: Duplicated content with no assertion change remains an alias with its original
106: lineage. Distinct same-date records need a supported ordering or a retained
107: conflict; IDs and filesystem order never break a semantic tie.
108: 
109: `original` returns eligible root assertions for each requested key. A later
110: revision is not an original merely because its root is unavailable. Multiple
111: unrelated roots remain competing assertions. `latest_available` returns eligible
112: assertions with no eligible admitted descendant superseding them for that key.
113: Only the eligible graph may remove an older record; future review acceptance
114: or later ingestion has no backward effect. If an eligible descendant's
115: intermediate ancestor is known but ineligible, the supported transitive edge
116: can still establish replacement; missing graph evidence cannot. Multiple live
117: leaves for the same exact quantity produce `CONFLICTING_VERSIONS` and remain
118: visible, rather than being averaged.
119: 
120: `harmonised` starts from latest eligible records and applies explicit admitted
121: transforms to a declared target basis, calendar and perimeter. Each transform
122: states input IDs, formula, authority/assumption support, review snapshot and
123: information dependencies. The output is a derived reading with a new ID;
124: original rows and their labels are preserved. Transform availability must pass
125: the same admission rules as other dependencies. Missing authority or a missing
126: opening bridge returns `HARMONISATION_UNSUPPORTED`; it does not relabel an old
127: number as comparable. An algebraic synthetic transform tests lineage only.
128: 
129: ## T5. Period arithmetic and change treatments
130: 
131: Subtract YTD A from longer YTD B only when concept, unit, currency, measurement
132: basis, perimeter, calendar, interval start, aggregation dimensions and evidence
133: revision compatibility match. The shorter interval must be a proper prefix.
134: The remainder is [A.end_exclusive, B.end_exclusive). Same vintage labels are
135: not sufficient if other context differs. Different vintage labels require a
136: specific admitted compatibility bridge covering the fact, not general claims
137: that both reports are audited. Original selection may return H1-v1 and FY-v2
138: for display while arithmetic between them is rejected.
139: 
140: The synthetic retained example is H1-v1 = 120, revised H1-v2 = 110, FY-v2 = 260.
141: Compatible subtraction gives H2-v2 = 260 - 110 = 150. Subtracting 120 gives
142: 140 arithmetically but fails the compatibility contract, so the financial query
143: returns no H2 value. A 52/53-week calendar change or a changed group perimeter
144: also requires an explicit bridge; endpoints alone cannot establish comparability.
145: 
146: An extraction correction changes the reading of unchanged source bytes; issuer
147: revision changes the reported assertion; an estimate update changes prospective
148: model inputs; a policy/error correction may require retrospective treatment.
149: The time contract requires change kind, authority/rule version, effective date,
150: affected periods/accounts, opening-balance bridge, transition method and review
151: evidence. It does not determine the standards-specific classification. Missing
152: authority returns `TREATMENT_UNSUPPORTED` and preserves original/revised views.
153: No fixture here certifies IFRS, US GAAP or ASBE retrospective/prospective rules;
154: P17 must supply the supporting methods and authoritative cases.
155: 
156: ## T6. Alternatives, invariants and interactions
157: 
158: Permissive fallback makes archival data easier to query but cannot establish
159: system history: two systems can ingest the same public filing on different
160: days. Strict receipt-based system admission and separately qualified exploration
161: are therefore proposed. Treating all missing publication data as fatal to every
162: operation would wrongly prevent a supported system-possession query. Combining
163: admission and revision selection in one “latest” switch hides these differences.
164: The proposed orthogonal fields make the requested historical claim explicit.
165: 
166: For date-only publication, start-of-day admission is convenient but admits
167: possible future information during that day. End-of-interval admission is
168: conservative and preserves the unknown instant. An arbitrary noon default is
169: unjustified. Exact research backtests requiring intraday availability must obtain
170: better evidence or leave those observations unresolved.
171: 
172: For every selection, selected and definitely excluded IDs are disjoint; selected
173: IDs can coexist with unresolved competitors, in which case the affected claim
174: is unresolved. Hashes and old views are immutable; a correction invalidates
175: current dependent results without rewriting historical records. Increasing K
176: may legitimately change latest selected values, so value monotonicity is not
177: an invariant. Eligibility grows only if the evidence/review snapshot and other
178: filters stay fixed. No view claims the issuer's assertions are economically true.
179: 
180: Evidence supplies immutable versions and layer decisions; history/uncertainty
181: must retain live alternatives; accounting supplies transition authority and
182: opening bridges; language/IR must serialize cutoffs, intervals and required
183: contexts; persistence supplies receipts, immutable snapshots and transitive
184: invalidation; interfaces expose unavailable versus unresolved versus unsupported
185: outcomes. Full definitions of those interfaces, identity/replay and domain
186: treatment remain later contracts. Human acceptance remains pending.
187: 
188: ## Revision record and fixture conventions
189: 
190: The current examples are `time-fixtures-v2.json`; v1 remains preserved.
191: The harmonisation example previously expected a conditional result despite an
192: empty request assumption list. That is wrong relative to E2's explicit-selection
193: rule. v2 selects `bridge-assumed`, records accepted synthetic review and byte
194: support for the transform, and names its information dependency. A transform
195: with an unselected required assumption must remain unresolved, with no admitted
196: derived value. An assumption ID is selected only by its presence in the request's
197: `assumptions` list; merely appearing in the transform does not select it.
198: 
199: Both harmonisation examples now use the documented `record_subset` operation.
200: Their restricted universe contains only h1-original; they illustrate a derived
201: reading of that source, not selection from a complete restatement history.
202: The all-record investor example still selects h1-restated and fy. Base review
203: acceptance assumes no later information dependencies beyond the stated bytes
204: and context. These explicit synthetic premises supply no historical-publication,
205: accounting-authority or issuer-restatement evidence.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/history-families-contract-v1.md
SHA256: 87140fbbbbd9137d792f22ed92e92d274a0a1cf9b2c332f2e743df2d92bcc3bd
1: # History families: proposed contract, version 1
2: 
3: This contract specifies how one set of disclosures can support several joint
4: financial histories. It addresses MC08–MC11 and G1/G16, with evidence, chronology,
5: language and reproducibility dependencies under MC02/MC04/MC05/MC18/MC25/MC27.
6: It is a proposed specification. Its examples are deliberately synthetic;
7: independent review, accounting validity and human acceptance remain pending.
8: The companion `history-families-fixtures-v1.json` contains specified inputs and
9: expected results, not observations from an implemented engine.
10: 
11: ## H1. Inputs and the question being answered
12: 
13: A reconstruction request supplies the following versioned records. References
14: mean immutable record versions and content hashes, not a mutable name such as
15: “latest”. A declared unknown differs from an omitted required field.
16: 
17: | Record | Required content | Admission consequence |
18: | --- | --- | --- |
19: | Evidence/time view | Source, reading, interpretation and review IDs; admission mode, selection mode, cutoffs, context-specific candidate-set revisions and unknown eligibility | Use the evidence v3 and time v2 rules. Unknown availability remains an affected-query obligation. |
20: | Observation | Concept, entity, perimeter version, economic instant or half-open reporting interval, calendar, basis, unit/currency, scale, sign and precision/rounding support; expression and source links | Two occurrences may refer to one quantity only through a supported context mapping. Never equate totals from different periods or bases by matching labels. |
21: | Interpretation and assumptions | Alternative IDs, conflict scope, admission layer, selected conditions, rationale, author and dependency versions; separate uncertainty category | Reading acceptance is not factual truth, treatment permission or a probability. An unselected assumption is not a constraint. |
22: | Opening state | Economic identities, rights/obligations, dated contract/lot/cohort state; accounting balances and reconciliation where supported; explicit unknown domains | Missing opening facts remain variables/obligations. Missing accounting support cannot be filled by balancing entries. |
23: | Model scope | Entities/perimeters, periods, candidate event/contract types, admissible domains, exclusions and reasons, template registry and mappings with versions | Finite slots or a bounded timeline are declared modeling restrictions, never evidence that excluded events did not occur. |
24: | Source obligations | Every in-scope observation/narrative obligation, applicability decision, dependent query and disposition | Unmapped, disputed, unsupported and not-yet-inspected content remains visible. An unexplained disposition prevents scope adequacy. |
25: | Query and budget | Requested layer, outputs, views/composition, scenario drivers and horizon, resolution, explicit resource ceilings, traversal policy and continuation reference | Omitted budget or view composition is a request error. Zero budget is valid; it is not an empty family. |
26: 
27: A template proposal must name its origin (source-supported, supplied assumption
28: or candidate requiring review), applicability conditions, economic state and event
29: effects, observation mappings, and accounting rules if it requests journals.
30: Each admitted constraint points to its exact support or selected assumption.
31: Unsupported accounting treatment is retained as an unresolved proposal with its
32: reason; it does not become an admissible treatment branch. Conversely, supported
33: reported-value arithmetic can proceed without claiming that a journal exists.
34: 
35: The output identifies its **target layer**. A conditional reported-value family
36: can be defined while the accounting-history family is unresolved. Calling both
37: simply “feasible” would conceal a different target. Any unresolved obligation
38: that can change membership in the requested family prevents an unconditional
39: admissibility claim, even if the currently enforced subset has a witness.
40: 
41: ## H2. Joint trajectories, views and identity
42: 
43: Let h contain the chosen interpretations, dated economic states, events and,
44: where admitted, journals and balances. Within declared scope M and immutable
45: view v, define F(M,v) as the set of h satisfying all admitted opening,
46: transition, context-mapping and observation constraints jointly, conditional
47: on the listed assumptions. A missing required constraint leaves a partial
48: formulation; the implementation must retain the obligation and label witnesses
49: of the relaxed formulation as candidates, not validated members of the target.
50: 
51: Shared economic IDs link contracts across periods and accounts. Observation
52: IDs identify assertions; they are not substitute contract IDs. Two assertions
53: about the same balance constrain one shared variable after context alignment.
54: A bridge connects successive states using the same events. For example, the
55: synthetic opening 100 plus an event amount 20 implies closing 120. A candidate
56: closing 110 fails that bridge even if 110 appears elsewhere as a possible total.
57: This small identity assumes no omitted flows or valuation effects; actual
58: bridges require the accounting and domain contracts.
59: 
60: For the principal example, let nonnegative amounts x and y denote two components
61: of one reported total in synthetic USD. The disclosure condition is x+y=100.
62: The marginal ranges are each [0,100], but x=y=100 is impossible because its sum
63: is 200. Projection onto x may return [0,100], retaining the parent-family ID and
64: an extension obligation: any downstream use must find a y satisfying the same
65: family. Joining independent marginal ranges discards that obligation.
66: 
67: A named view is a predicate on the parent family, not another independent
68: history. Write A={h in F: x>=40} and B={h in F: x<=60}. Their intersection has
69: 40<=x<=60 and y=100-x; their union is F. The overlap is not counted twice.
70: A request with several view IDs must say union or intersection. Views can also
71: leave parts of F uncovered. They provide no probability weights. Composition
72: across different family/view versions returns `FAMILY_CONTEXT_MISMATCH` unless
73: an explicit versioned alignment transformation proves the intended relation.
74: 
75: Family representation identity includes the full immutable semantic manifest:
76: view/review versions and candidate membership, scope, variables/domains,
77: constraints/support, selected assumptions, template/mapping versions and
78: resolution. Changing those fields creates a new family version. Merely changing
79: a search budget or traversal does not. A query has its own identity including
80: the family version, requested outputs, composition, layer, scenarios and horizon.
81: 
82: An individual witness identifies its family, complete assignments and dated
83: trajectory, interpretation choices and provenance. Two labels attached to the
84: same assignment do not create two witnesses. Different provenance manifests can
85: represent equal economic trajectories without sharing representation identity;
86: economic equivalence needs a stated comparison domain and argument. No solver
87: normal form or content hash proves that equivalence. A symbolic region is not
88: one witness and cannot be assigned one vote in a distribution.
89: 
90: For E05, an explicit conditional selector k in {A,B} gives
91: (k=A and revenue=120) or (k=B and revenue=110), with cash=40. These are assumptions
92: about which conflicting source to use, not evidence of a source's correctness.
93: The unconditioned reported-value response remains unresolved with both values;
94: the ratios conditional on A and B are 3 and 11/4. The target is not
95: revenue=120 and revenue=110. Choosing that conjunction would compute an empty
96: set for a different question. Missing treatment authority blocks journals in
97: both branches. Unknown source eligibility also propagates; constructing a
98: conditional branch cannot bypass time admission. E05 still lacks a qualifying
99: real-source conflict; this example does not repair that source gap.
100: 
101: ## H3. Construction, search and continuation
102: 
103: The proposed interfaces are staged so that each decision has an accountable
104: input and an inspectable output. They do not select a solver or implement it.
105: 
106: | Operation | Input | Output and stopping rule |
107: | --- | --- | --- |
108: | ProposeCandidates | Reviewed view, opening state, scope and versioned template registry | Candidate instances, applicability reasons, exclusions and source-obligation dispositions. No arbitrary-disclosure discovery claim. |
109: | AdmitConstraints | Candidate instances plus category-specific evidence/assumption/treatment decisions | Supported constraints with origin IDs, rejected proposals with reasons, unresolved obligations and target-layer completeness. Stop target-dependent admission on missing authority. |
110: | BuildFamily | Admitted constraints, domains and full dependency manifest | Family formula/representation, shared symbol map, search partition specification, unresolved formulation obligations. Reject duplicate/conflicting IDs, missing context or undeclared symbols. |
111: | ValidateWitness | Family version and full candidate assignment/trajectory | Satisfied constraints with checked support, violated constraints, unsupported checks or missing fields. Valid only when every required target constraint is checked. |
112: | SearchFamily | Family version, explicit budget, capabilities, traversal and optional receipt | Evidence about regions, witnesses, infeasibility reasons/proofs, unresolved regions and a continuation receipt. A resource stop preserves the original formula. |
113: | QueryFamily | Family plus requested view/projection/scenario/resolution | Set/region or conditional results with completeness and dependency status. Bounds from explored regions are labelled explored-only. |
114: 
115: Source obligations use `mapped`, `not_applicable_with_reason`, `out_of_scope`,
116: `disputed` or `unresolved`. `Mapped` requires the actual constraint/support IDs;
117: several assertions can support one constraint without double-counting evidence.
118: `Not applicable` requires a reason and review version, not just a count.
119: Out-of-scope disclosures remain on the coverage report and wider mission queue.
120: A source count equal to a constraint count establishes none of these relations.
121: P17 must supply domain-specific candidate generation and applicability methods.
122: 
123: Search may partition a family into reproducible decision cells. Each cell
124: retains the original family formula AND its decision assignment/predicate.
125: A declared finite choice needs an explicit domain; continuous quantities remain
126: regions. The partition manifest states why its cells are exhaustive and
127: nonoverlapping. Named views are not automatically cells. If the backend cannot
128: establish a partition, retain the original region as unresolved instead of
129: inventing a finite-universe completeness claim.
130: 
131: For the examples, a cell key is the tuple (partition-version, decision-ID,
132: value-ID), or a sorted tuple of these assignments for several decisions. Decision
133: and value IDs come from the immutable manifest, never array positions or
134: traversal order. The retained key and manifest uniquely reconstruct the cell;
135: a digest can index them. General byte encoding belongs to language/IR. A search
136: receipt records the exact manifest, explicit traversal order, examined keys,
137: unvisited keys or a reconstructible range predicate, formulas, resource use,
138: backend/version and unresolved reasons. Ordinal suffixes are valid only with
139: the pinned ordering/domain manifest; changing order cannot reuse an old suffix.
140: 
141: A cell has one of three coverage dispositions: represented feasible region with
142: validated witness or another supported existence argument; proved infeasible
143: with checked contradiction/proof and scope; unresolved, retaining the region
144: and reason (`unvisited`, `unknown`, `unsupported`, `interrupted`, `cancelled`).
145: A feasible continuous region is preserved symbolically: one sample never
146: exhausts its points. “Every declared cell decided” means the whole family is
147: represented within M, not that every history was enumerated. All-cell
148: infeasibility proves emptiness only for the complete admitted formulation and
149: an exhaustive partition. Unsupported required operations or unresolved source
150: obligations veto that stronger conclusion.
151: 
152: The response therefore reports three independent fields: `existence` (witness,
153: proved_empty, unknown); `declared_search` (complete, partial, not_started);
154: `economic_scope` (supported_for_declared_scope, unresolved, explicitly_limited).
155: A scope may be explicitly limited even when search is complete. A partial
156: formulation also reports `formulation_complete=false`; its candidates do not
157: set target `existence=witness`. These statuses are scoped to the requested layer.
158: No value of these fields alone certifies whole-company coverage.
159: 
160: Resume requires exact semantic-manifest and partition identity. A changed
161: budget can continue unresolved cells. Changed evidence, assumptions, domains,
162: rules or partition create a new request and prevent blind continuation. Preserve
163: already committed partial work on cancellation; if a cell's result was not
164: committed, leave that whole cell unresolved. A zero-budget request returns the
165: entire original region unresolved and no proof of emptiness. No new solver is
166: required to specify this behavior.
167: 
168: ## H4. Economic memory, grouping and refinement
169: 
170: A historical accounting balance does not describe all future contractual cash
171: flows. A principal of 100 with a fixed rate of 5% and a principal of 100 whose
172: floating rate was 5% can produce the same stipulated historical interest of 5.
173: If the next full-year rate is 7%, future interest is respectively 5 and 7. A
174: principal due next year and one due a year later can likewise have identical
175: current balances but different principal repayments within a one-year query.
176: The histories must retain rate/reset terms and maturity even when their
177: historical postings happen to agree. The example establishes arithmetic under
178: specified simple-interest assumptions, not a recognition or valuation method.
179: 
180: Required state is determined by requested scenarios, horizon and outputs plus
181: the applicable domain methods. It includes legal entity/perimeter, ownership and
182: rights, contract/lot/cohort identities, currency, principal/units, recognition
183: and measurement basis where applicable, rates/resets, schedules/maturities,
184: options/guarantees/collateral and linked dependencies whenever those affect the
185: query. “Unknown” is a value status requiring preservation; absence of a field
186: cannot silently mean no guarantee or no option. Later banks, insurance, groups,
187: FX, securities and cross-standard methods must extend these state requirements;
188: a scalar balance schema is not a whole-product substitute.
189: 
190: A grouping request names constituents, transformation/version, retained detail,
191: scenario domain, horizon, outputs, rules and exact/approximate claim. An exact
192: certificate must show that aggregation G commutes with every admitted transition
193: T for that domain, G(T(s,u))=T_group(G(s),u), and that requested outputs satisfy
194: O(s)=O_group(G(s)). These are proof obligations, not general facts about
195: financial balances. Missing proof/evaluation returns `GROUPING_UNSUPPORTED`;
196: it leaves detailed histories available and cannot delete them. A certificate is
197: part of query/resolution identity, with constituent and rule versions.
198: 
199: For the deliberately narrow simple-interest fixture, two principals 40 and 60
200: share entity, currency, a fixed 1/20 annual rate, a common day-count convention,
201: no intermediate principal change, and maturities after the half-year horizon.
202: Only total principal and interest are requested. Individual interest is
203: 40*(1/20)*(1/2)+60*(1/20)*(1/2)=1+3/2=5/2. Grouping gives
204: (40+60)*(1/20)*(1/2)=5/2 by distributivity. Principal remains 100 under both
205: representations; any stipulated interest-payment cash transition subtracts the
206: same 5/2. This proves the named aggregate outputs/transitions for those
207: assumptions, not contract-level cash flows or other accounting measurements.
208: Constituent records remain linked so a later query can refine the group.
209: 
210: A changed output (individual contract exposure), rate/reset/maturity/guarantee
211: assumption, rule or horizon requires a new sufficiency decision. Reusing the old
212: certificate returns `RESOLUTION_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED`. Refinement produces a
213: new resolution version linked to its parent and constituent detail; if the
214: required detail was never preserved, return `DETAIL_UNAVAILABLE`, not fabricated
215: lots or contracts. Refinement can restore represented detail, not recover facts
216: that were never known.
217: 
218: Approximation is a distinct, optional future claim. Its record must name each
219: output, unit, scenario/horizon domain, absolute/relative tolerance, justification,
220: error argument or evaluation evidence, and exact-detail fallback. No tolerance,
221: empirical error bound or approximate grouping is approved by this contract.
222: Changing the query invalidates the approximation's applicability. Counts of
223: similar contracts and similarity of old postings are insufficient evidence.
224: 
225: ## H5. Revisions, errors and layer interactions
226: 
227: Current eligibility depends on the candidate population as well as selected
228: assertions. Adding a competing source to an unchanged selection key creates a
229: new membership revision and invalidates affected family/query descendants.
230: Changed interpretation review, assumptions, template applicability, opening state
231: or treatment rules similarly changes the relevant semantic manifest. An old
232: result remains readable under its frozen view with a historical label. It must
233: not be relabelled current. Reuse requires a complete dependency comparison or an
234: explicitly supported independence argument; missing dependency information
235: means reuse is unestablished. An unrelated cash-only family can be reusable
236: while a revenue family is invalidated, subject to the same time/cutoff checks.
237: 
238: | Outcome | Required payload and consequence |
239: | --- | --- |
240: | `REQUEST_INVALID` / `CONTEXT_INCOMPLETE` | Missing fields, duplicate IDs or ambiguous composition with record locations; no admitted family is fabricated. |
241: | `CONSTRAINT_VIOLATION` | Candidate, failed original constraint and arithmetic/logic; candidate rejection is not family emptiness. |
242: | `PROVED_INFEASIBLE` | Complete target/formula, region, supported exhaustive scope and checked contradiction/proof; no conclusions about out-of-scope real histories. |
243: | `UNKNOWN` | Undecided formula and reason, even if no witness was returned. |
244: | `OPERATION_UNSUPPORTED` / `TREATMENT_UNSUPPORTED` | Exact operation or missing authority, affected target and retained formula/proposal; unsupported does not mean false. |
245: | `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` / `CANCELLED` | Committed region results, all unresolved cells/regions and continuation receipt; partial valid witnesses survive. |
246: | `STALE_CONTINUATION` / `STALE_VIEW` | Expected and supplied immutable versions; require a new request or explicit frozen access, with no mutation of historical records. |
247: | `RESOLUTION_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` | Changed query/rule/dependency fields and retained constituents; no use of an inapplicable certificate. |
248: 
249: These names specify distinctions for this contract. The later failure contract
250: must reconcile a common encoding with evidence/time/E05; this is not a claim
251: that a production exception hierarchy exists. A per-region outcome and a
252: request termination reason may coexist. For example, one feasible cell and
253: cancellation leave a partial family, not a cancelled mathematical truth.
254: 
255: | Neighbor | History-family input or output | Responsibility remaining outside this contract |
256: | --- | --- | --- |
257: | Evidence/time | Consume `SelectedEvidenceView`, contextual predicates, conflicts, membership revisions and unresolved eligibility; return dependency/obligation IDs | Complete source support and exact temporal admission belong to evidence v3/time v2. History construction cannot relax them. |
258: | Uncertainty | Consume category-labelled admission/selected assumptions; return joint family, conditional branch IDs and unresolved regions | Specify narrowing, elections, estimates, revisions and defensible joint measures. No equal weights or normalization over successful cells by default. |
259: | Accounting | Consume dated rule applicability, treatment constraints and opening/event bridges; return economic/history candidates plus unsupported obligations | Establish authority, recognition, measurement, journals and statements. Reported-value or economic feasibility cannot imply compliant accounting. |
260: | Language/IR | Supply shared identities, complete contexts, typed predicates, region/proof/status and source-location requirements | Define portable encoding, parser/typed authoring semantics, numerical capability and migration; symbolic prose is not an implemented IR. |
261: | Persistence | Supply immutable manifests, witness/region IDs, continuation receipts and dependency edges including membership | Define atomic commits, stale-write handling and reverse invalidation. Failed persistence cannot silently mark an unfinished cell complete. |
262: | Interfaces | Supply query layer, view composition, assumptions, scope/exclusions, coverage and unresolved outputs | Display those distinctions and allow conditional/frozen queries; avoid a single “complete” badge. |
263: | Failure semantics | Supply the outcome distinctions above with causal IDs and partial results | Harmonize names, retryability and recovery behavior without conflating mathematical and operational failures. |
264: 
265: ## H6. Matched alternatives and proposed choices
266: 
267: Hold the same synthetic inputs x>=0, y>=0, x+y=100 and the same requested
268: outputs fixed: the joint feasible set, membership of (50,50) and (100,100),
269: and the A/B views from H2. A joint symbolic representation retains the line
270: segment, includes (50,50), rejects (100,100), and expresses overlap directly.
271: It is proposed because it preserves these meanings. Merely writing a formula
272: does not prove that construction captured all disclosures or that a backend can
273: solve it. An unsupported query still has to return its original region.
274: 
275: Explicit finite history enumeration is exact for an explicitly finite universe.
276: For example, if a separately justified scope limits x to {40,70}, enumerating
277: (40,60) and (70,30) represents that narrower family exactly. Under the original
278: continuous inputs, listing endpoints (0,100) and (100,0) alone misses (50,50).
279: An honest enumerator must report those as witnesses and leave the remainder
280: unresolved; it cannot change the input domain merely to report completeness.
281: Finite enumeration remains a suitable implementation candidate for genuinely
282: finite subproblems, with the same joint constraints and source obligations.
283: 
284: Independent account intervals [0,100] and [0,100] answer marginal questions, but
285: their Cartesian product admits (100,100). Using that product as the joint family
286: is wrong relative to the specified target. Retaining the linking equation with
287: the intervals repairs it, making them a presentation of the joint constraint
288: rather than an independent reconstruction. The proposed representation may
289: therefore expose intervals as summaries with their parent relation preserved.
290: No speed, usability or stochastic ranking is inferred from these examples.
291: 
292: | Choice and status | Provenance and justification | Failure mode and smallest diagnostic |
293: | --- | --- | --- |
294: | Joint constraints, proposed semantic requirement | MC08 and P15 audit; H2 proves the dependence counterexample | Independent marginal combination; HF01/HF02 reject it. |
295: | Region plus search evidence, proposed interface | MC09; historical P04 illustrates but does not authorize the design | Witness promoted to completeness; HF04/HF06/HF07 distinguish them. |
296: | Explicit scope/obligation report, proposed interface | G1/P15; supplied templates cannot discover omitted events | Complete cell count hides exclusions; HF05/HF09 preserve them. |
297: | Stable manifest and cell keys, proposed engineering choice | MC25 reproducibility; semantic keys survive traversal changes | Order-dependent replay; HF07/HF15 reject mismatched continuation. Byte encoding remains IR work. |
298: | Exact rational small examples, fixture convenience | Deliberately stipulated numbers make algebra independently checkable | Mistaken universal decimal/solver policy; HF04 preserves strict endpoints, accounting/numerics remain open. |
299: | Finite example decisions and cell budgets, fixture convenience | Two branches expose interruption with a tiny example | Whole-company or continuous completeness inferred from counts; H3 requires preserved regions and scope. |
300: | Query-dependent grouping, proposed interface | MC11/G16; H4 derives a narrow positive case and counterexamples | Same postings mistaken for same exposure; HF10–HF12 require detail/revalidation. |
301: 
302: Z3, Python, rational arithmetic, Boolean truth-cell expansion, slot limits,
303: traversal order, storage and compression are not chosen as whole-product defaults.
304: Historical `reconstruction-profile-v1.md` and `reconstruction_v1.py` (construction
305: and grouping functions) are bounded prototype evidence. P03's authored debt
306: lowering likewise does not establish disclosure-to-template discovery. The
307: current contract's arguments are the explicit set and arithmetic derivations
308: above; no uninspected paper or accounting authority is invoked.
309: 
310: ## H7. Completion and remaining decisions
311: 
312: The companion fixtures cover joint dependence, contexts, source conflict,
313: continuous regions, scope, honest failure/search, economic memory, grouping,
314: revision invalidation and frozen access. Their algebra and file consistency can
315: be checked without building a product module. They do not establish semantic
316: completeness of this specification; that requires independent result review.
317: 
318: P15-E09 (partial outcomes), E11 (numerical capabilities), E12 (economic memory),
319: E13 (scenarios/weights), and E05 (layered review) now have concrete proposed
320: history-family consequences. They remain open pending counterpart contracts and
321: review. All fourteen P15 decisions and all MC01–MC35/G1–G18 obligations remain
322: active. In particular, sufficient state for every sector, source-grounded
323: candidate generation, dated accounting permissions, general grouping proofs,
324: and probability/calibration methods belong to further P16/P17 work. This slice
325: does not select the initial product profile or dependency order.
326: 
327: The stop condition is saved H1–H7, specified fixtures, architecture registration,
328: actual local/registered-check records, and explicit residuals. The next task is
329: a separately reviewed uncertainty contract. Pending E05/history-family bounded
330: result review and expected whole-architecture check failure do not prevent that
331: planning or authorised independent drafting. Full independent P16 result review
332: remains required before phase acceptance.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/uncertainty-contract-v1.md
SHA256: 77010c686d06d398b60e6fa861089f54ec628571770ff7eab12cea4194a7ed0a
1: # Integrated uncertainty operations — proposed contract v1
2: 
3: This specification completes the operation layer above uncertainty admission,
4: joint-measure attachment and history-family construction. It addresses MC08–MC14,
5: MC18, MC25, MC27 and MC33–MC35, principally G1, G6, G15 and G16. Its finite examples
6: are authored assumptions. They establish neither company probabilities nor an
7: accounting treatment. Product implementation and independent acceptance remain
8: pending. The initial profile and all fourteen P15 decisions remain open.
9: 
10: ## 1. Question, evidence and default audit
11: 
12: Can a retained family support conditioning, new evidence and conditional outcomes
13: without invented weights, repeated information or discarded unresolved regions?
14: The baseline is the proposed admission U1–U6, attachment v2 A–F and history H1–H7
15: contracts. Core 0's uncertainty section and chapter 5's finite-model derivation
16: are mathematical design evidence, not approved product behavior. This document
17: extends their operation semantics; earlier supplements' deferrals describe their
18: historical scope. Their unchanged records and failure examples remain applicable.
19: 
20: The pass criterion for this bounded task is a coherent specified exchange and
21: independently recomputable finite examples, with preserved identities and failure
22: receipts. Invalid arithmetic, lost dependencies or contradictory state assertions
23: veto this document's internal check and trigger repair. Missing empirical support
24: vetoes an empirical claim, not continued specification. Checker counts and example
25: coverage are explanatory only. No stochastic experiment or architecture ranking
26: is performed. Results are retained in uncertainty-fixtures-v1.json and the P16
27: result note with fresh check records. Stop after this group; accounting is next.
28: 
29: | Choice and provenance | Reason; misleading failure | Early diagnostic; status |
30: | --- | --- | --- |
31: | Optional law, MC12 and attachment B | Facts and feasible alternatives need not have weights; uniform branch weights would invent information. | Unweighted fixture forbids aggregate mean/quantile; proposed invariant. |
32: | Immutable linked records, evidence/time/history contracts | Corrections must preserve earlier answers; pointer substitution would leak later evidence. | Parent hashes and explicit cutoff checks; provisional representation choice. |
33: | Exact finite rational examples, approved plan | Independently check normalization; finite success could be mistaken for continuous inference. | Fractions recomputation plus unsupported point-conditioning example; fixture convenience only. |
34: | Observation blocks, chapter 5 and extraction identity rules | Capture reused information; different files or IDs could disguise one observation. | Repetition, components, alternative extraction and correction examples; proposed invariant. |
35: | Dependence declared, never inferred from absence of links | Multiplying factors requires a conditional model; unjustified independence overstates information. | Unknown overlap rejects independent multiplication; proposed invariant. |
36: | Preserve every requested region, history H4 and chapter 5 | Solver failure is not an economic outcome; successful-only means would change the question. | Partial mass and explicit completion-condition examples; proposed invariant. |
37: 
38: Skeptical audit: the comparison fixes semantic inputs; neither prior prototypes,
39: branch counts nor execution success define the baseline or promotion rule. There
40: is no runtime/environment comparison. Continuous inference and causal evaluation
41: remain explicit capability limits, not evidence against those research directions.
42: 
43: ## 2. Exchange, identities and validation
44: 
45: An immutable `UncertaintyRequest` has `request_id`, `version`, `predecessor`,
46: `operation`, `family_ref`, `view_ref`, `attachment_ref` (explicit null if absent),
47: `model_ref` (explicit null if unweighted), `time_ref`, `evidence_view_ref`,
48: `admission_ref`, `purpose`, `information_cutoff`, `evidence_cutoff`, `query`,
49: `dependencies` and `provenance`. References bind exact `id@version` records;
50: payload changes require a new version or identity. Time mode, publication and
51: byte-vintage eligibility follow T1–T3; a new observation can produce a later
52: view only with an explicit new time/admission record. Frozen earlier views remain
53: queryable. Source occurrence, semantic assertion, interpretation, observation
54: block and likelihood factor have separate identities.
55: 
56: The query declares the operation-specific condition, evidence update or outcome
57: selection, including scenario/path/date and typed quantities. Dependencies include
58: the transitive closure of all typed record references, including assumptions,
59: review selection, law, accounting method and source lineage. An empty selection
60: never means all. Outcome labels such as `a` are domain members, not record keys.
61: The fixture registry is self-contained: it instantiates this exchange without
62: silently importing the predecessor fixtures' registry. File references pin source
63: contracts by SHA-256; they do not count as observation blocks.
64: 
65: `UncertaintyResult` retains request identity, old/proposed view, disposition,
66: reason codes, support and preserved records, unresolved obligations, future
67: dependencies, `family_effect`, payload, and separate statuses:
68: 
69: * `structural_validity`: valid/invalid;
70: * `mathematical_status`: satisfied/failed/undefined/unresolved;
71: * `empirical_justification`: stipulated/not_established/supported_for_named_scope;
72: * `capability_status`: specified_only_no_runtime/unsupported;
73: * `computation_status`: specified_exact/specified_partial/symbolic/not_computed.
74: 
75: Fixture computations are expected outputs, never runtime success claims.
76: Accounting validity and human acceptance are separate fields, neither inferred
77: from mathematical status. Successful derivation creates a candidate child view;
78: it preserves the parent and changes no active view pointer. Admission/selection
79: is a separate explicit operation. Rejection stores submitted bytes and a receipt
80: but creates no child view, posterior, aggregate result or partial domain write.
81: `state_before` and `state_after` name the same selected family/view/attachment
82: and parent hashes on rejection. Receipt storage itself is not a domain update.
83: Retrying identical request bytes returns the same logical receipt; a reused
84: identity with different bytes fails `IDENTITY_CONFLICT`. Actual atomic storage,
85: crash recovery and concurrency are obligations for the later persistence contract.
86: 
87: | Validation stage | Invalid or unresolved condition | Disposition and consequence |
88: | --- | --- | --- |
89: | Envelope/registry | Missing field, scalar where reference array required, identity collision, unresolved reference | REQUEST_INVALID; record exact field/reference and preserve submission. |
90: | Binding/admission | Wrong family/view/model, event outside domain, incompatible unit/purpose, missing dependency, inadmissible cutoff | REQUEST_INVALID; no substitution of nearest/newest record. |
91: | Law/event | Missing masses, negative/nonfinite mass, nonunit total, unresolved measurability | Invalid laws fail mathematics; unresolved obligations remain symbolic; no computed probability. |
92: | Evidence reuse | Repeated information, unmodeled overlap, correction applied atop superseded evidence | REQUEST_INVALID with the specific reuse code; old law retained. |
93: | Normalization | Zero denominator/normalizer | UNDEFINED; undefined mathematics, no posterior. |
94: | Capability | No admitted law, unavailable evaluator, continuous point without conditional model | UNSUPPORTED or RETAINED_SYMBOLIC; preserve feasible family and expressions. |
95: | Outcome coverage | Unsupported method, numerical failure, unexplored domain | PARTIAL; retain region and mass/unknown mass, never silently condition on completion. |
96: 
97: These stages reuse the saved attachment/malformed supplements. Error lists are
98: ordered by stage then record key; later numerical evaluation does not run after
99: an earlier invalid prerequisite. Preserve all unresolved references in receipts.
100: 
101: ## 3. Conditioning and scenarios
102: 
103: An observational condition names an admitted event A, the parent law P, the
104: information used to define A and its role. For finite elementary outcomes S,
105: require a measurable A subset of S and P(A)>0. Define
106: 
107:     P_A(s) = P(s) 1{s in A} / P(A),
108:     P(B | A) = sum_{s in A intersect B} P(s) / P(A).
109: 
110: The numerator sums elementary outcomes once even when display labels overlap.
111: The child retains S and records zero mass outside A, the event identity,
112: denominator and parent law. Keeping zero-mass members distinguishes the declared
113: domain from its positive-mass support. Reapplying A is mathematically idempotent;
114: it records no new information factor. If A came from observation block D, the
115: child's consumed-information set includes D and its semantic lineage, preventing
116: subsequent likelihood multiplication by the same information. A hard constraint
117: already used to construct the family is likewise consumed, even if it did not
118: appear as a numerical likelihood. Evidence used to fit a prior/model is declared
119: in that model's training/calibration lineage for the same check.
120: 
121: With masses a=1/2, b=1/3, c=1/6, A={a,b}, B={b,c}, the denominator is 5/6,
122: intersection mass is 1/3, and P(B|A)=2/5. The conditional masses are (3/5,2/5,0).
123: P(A)=0 returns `ZERO_CONDITION_MASS` and no child; an empty feasible subset in
124: an unweighted family is instead scoped infeasibility. An unweighted nonempty
125: restriction yields a feasible subset with `probability=null`, not a distribution.
126: 
127: An exogenous stress changes a declared driver/scenario while retaining history
128: weights unless an explicit observation model says that observing the scenario
129: provides information. Its result is labeled scenario-assumed, not P(.|observed
130: scenario). A structural intervention requires a causal model, modified equations,
131: intervened variables and an identification/validation argument; `do(X=x)` is
132: never lowered to filtering `X=x`. This proposal retains such requests as
133: unsupported until a declared evaluator and its evidence are supplied in P17.
134: 
135: For continuous/mixed laws, event conditioning uses the same measure formula when
136: the event has positive mass and the evaluator is supported. A continuous point
137: event of zero mass requires an explicit regular conditional kernel/version (or
138: justified reduced parameterization) with the conditioning variable, base measure,
139: support and chosen version at the queried point. A density value is not the
140: probability of that point. No epsilon window or Gaussian penalty is invented.
141: General existence, uniqueness at null points and numerical evaluation remain
142: method obligations; symbolic expressions may be retained without a numeric answer.
143: 
144: ## 4. Evidence updates and information reuse
145: 
146: An `ObservationBlock` records its immutable identity, purpose, observed values and
147: full financial context, source occurrences and assertion/reading references,
148: `information_atoms`, relations (`same_information`, `component_of`, `derived_from`,
149: `supersedes`), availability/time view, admission and dependencies. Information atoms
150: name semantic observations, not bytes: a duplicated PDF, a new extraction or a
151: repeated table entry does not automatically supply new information. Where semantic
152: equivalence cannot be decided, overlap is `unknown`; new IDs alone cannot prove
153: independence. No general automatic duplicate detector is claimed.
154: 
155: A `LikelihoodModel` records target family/coordinates, law purpose, observation
156: block(s), base measure for observed data, conditional arguments, parameter version,
157: units, support, consumed information and justification. Its output is a
158: nonnegative measurable L(s). For discrete observations L is a probability of the
159: specified observation; for continuous observations it can be a density and may
160: exceed one. Extraction confidence, expert preference and solver success are not
161: likelihoods. A finite update requires finite nonnegative values on all S and
162: 
163:     Z = sum_s P(s)L(s) > 0;    P_new(s) = P(s)L(s)/Z.
164: 
165: The result records unnormalized masses, Z, the complete posterior including zeros,
166: new consumed-information set and parent view. With P=(1/2,1/3,1/6) and
167: L=(1/2,1,0), unnormalized masses are (1/4,1/3,0), Z=7/12 and posterior
168: (3/7,4/7,0). If Z=0, return `ZERO_NORMALIZER`; do not substitute equal weights or
169: silently broaden support. A later observation inconsistent with every positive-mass
170: history calls for an explicit model/family revision, not an invisible numerical fix.
171: 
172: Two observation blocks can enter one joint likelihood L(D1,D2|s). Sequential
173: updating must use L(D2|D1,s); replacing this by L(D2|s) requires an explicit
174: conditional-independence assumption with target-specific justification. Different
175: information atoms are necessary bookkeeping, not a proof of independence. A
176: dependent conditional model may legitimately reuse D1 as a conditioning argument;
177: it must name D1 as already consumed and add only D2 as incremental information.
178: Unknown dependence blocks the independent-factor route while retaining the blocks
179: for a later joint model. Marginal likelihood tables alone do not define the joint
180: likelihood.
181: 
182: | Submission | Required result |
183: | --- | --- |
184: | Same immutable request/bytes | Idempotent receipt lookup, no second child or factor. |
185: | New request, same source occurrence/information block | `DUPLICATE_INFORMATION`; preserve prior posterior. |
186: | Total plus all its components as independent factors | `DERIVED_INFORMATION_REUSE`; one joint block or an explicit conditional model is required. A deterministic total adds no independent information beyond all components. |
187: | Alternative extraction of one source span | `ALTERNATE_READING_NOT_NEW_OBSERVATION`; resolve/branch the reading under admission U rules. A new noisy measurement model must be explicitly justified if intended. |
188: | Potentially overlapping blocks with unknown relation | `DEPENDENCE_UNRESOLVED`; no independent update. |
189: | Corrected reading multiplied into an old posterior | `CORRECTION_REQUIRES_REBASE`; retract/supersede in a new evidence view and recompute from a retained base preceding the affected block. |
190: 
191: A correction supplies the superseded block, corrected block, base view and the
192: ordered retained incremental updates to replay. Validate that none of the old
193: block's descendants survives without revalidation. Recompute from the base with
194: the corrected likelihood and any still-admissible downstream conditional factors.
195: Do not divide by the old likelihood: it may be zero and its support restriction
196: may already have removed alternatives. The old posterior, evidence view and
197: receipt remain unchanged. The illustrative corrected L=(1,1/2,1) on the original
198: P gives Z=5/6 and (3/5,1/5,1/5). This is a newly specified view, not historical
199: knowledge rewritten in place. Full crash-safe replay is a persistence obligation.
200: 
201: ## 5. Conditional outcome payloads
202: 
203: A query selects exact family/view, condition, scenario, path set, dates, accounting
204: methods and output quantities. Each row key is `(history_ref, condition_ref,
205: scenario_ref, path_ref, date)`. Cells carry entity/perimeter, concept, dimensions,
206: currency/unit/scale/sign, instant or interval and measurement basis, together with
207: state/journal/report references, assumptions and dependencies. A joint statement
208: vector shares its path and state references; marginals from incompatible histories
209: cannot be recombined into a synthetic balance sheet. Expected statements need not
210: describe any admissible history and remain summaries, not journal postings.
211: 
212: Cell status is `exact`, `symbolic`, `sampled`, `certified_bound`, `explored_range`
213: or `unresolved`. Exact means exact under the declared model and inputs. Sampled
214: requires sampler/seed/stream, weighting and uncertainty metadata; it is not exact.
215: Certified bounds require a cited proof/certificate, domain and endpoint semantics;
216: explored ranges describe only visited regions. Symbolic values retain expression,
217: domain and unresolved dependencies. No computational tag asserts accounting
218: eligibility or empirical forecast validity.
219: 
220: For weighted finite reporting, allocate every elementary outcome once to a
221: disjoint partition `completed`, `unsupported`, `numerical_failure`,
222: `solver_unknown`, `cancelled` or `unexplored`. Coverage masses total one (or the
223: explicit parent event mass for an unnormalized subquery); unknown mass stays
224: unknown and prevents a false exact total. Economic default is an economic-state
225: flag orthogonal to this partition: a fully computed default belongs to completed;
226: an unsupported default branch can remain unresolved. Report default mass separately
227: with its overlap with completion so it is never added twice. Default retains legal
228: obligations and usable state. Stop only affected descendants when a required method
229: is unsupported, retaining independent quantities through their dependencies.
230: 
231: Suppose a,b have exact expense 10,20 and c's valuation is unsupported. Under
232: P=(1/2,1/3,1/6), completed mass is 5/6, unresolved mass 1/6 and the completed
233: unnormalized contribution is 35/3. The unconditional mean and quantiles are
234: unresolved; 35/3 is not the mean of the full outcome. A separately requested
235: `condition_on_completion` query reports event {a,b}, denominator 5/6 and mean 14.
236: Its labels state that this is a computational selection, not an economic forecast
237: condition or evidence update. This view cannot silently feed back into historical
238: probabilities. Partial values may support bounds only with explicit bounds for
239: the missing region; a missing value is neither zero nor default.
240: 
241: When history weights are absent, retain the feasible set and separate within-history
242: future laws K(h,scenario) if available. No uniform averaging across histories.
243: Without a within-history law retain a deterministic/symbolic feasible outcome set.
244: A complete finite set {10,20,30} has exact range [10,30] but no mean or quantile.
245: If only {10,20} was explored and c is unresolved, [10,20] is merely an explored
246: range and cannot become a full-family bound. Even when every K is valid, an
247: envelope of their quantiles is not a predictive distribution. An optional history
248: law plus a measurable kernel defines a mixture; discrete reporting sums disjoint
249: history/innovation atoms, and continuous mixtures require a supported integrator.
250: 
251: ## 6. Interactions, alternatives and acceptance boundary
252: 
253: Evidence supplies contextual assertions and observation lineage; time determines
254: admissible information; admission separates the six uncertainty categories. History
255: families supply compatible joint states, scope, residual regions and refinement
256: maps. Accounting consumes a history/scenario/path and returns typed states and
257: journals with eligibility status; uncertainty never makes an unsupported treatment
258: admissible by giving it positive probability. Language/IR must type conditions,
259: measures, units, model purpose and requested aggregation; implicit casts from sets
260: to distributions are forbidden. Interfaces expose denominators, unresolved mass,
261: view lineage and evidence reuse receipts. Persistence retains immutable requests,
262: models and child derivations with resumable frontiers; failure semantics propagate
263: dependency-scoped unknowns. These are requirements on unfinished counterpart
264: contracts, not evidence that they are complete.
265:
