266: The two credible storage alternatives are a law linked to a family registry and
267: a self-contained joint model carrying its definitions. Compare them with identical
268: S, typed assignments, masses, evidence blocks, cutoffs, parent lineage and queries.
269: Under a bijection f of elementary outcomes require equal assignments and
270: P_embedded(x)=P_linked(f(x)); also preserve event sets, likelihood values and consumed
271: information. Finite conditioning and normalization then have equal sums by a change
272: of index. Registry links simplify shared revisions but require dependency closure;
273: self-contained packages simplify portable inspection but risk copied evidence being
274: counted twice. Both must retain semantic observation identities. A paired fixture
275: specifies the same conditional and posterior values in both forms; no throughput,
276: memory, empirical or general continuous-equivalence conclusion follows.
277: 
278: The provisional preference is explicit immutable shared identities with a
279: self-contained export containing the same identities and definitions. It follows
280: from traceable revisions and reuse, not from prototype convenience. Ownership and
281: initial-profile selection remain open. Weighted and unweighted reporting answer
282: different questions and are not competing estimators to rank on these examples.
283: Single joint likelihood and conditional sequential factorization are both credible
284: when their models match; naive multiplication is valid only under the stated
285: conditional independence. Correction by replay is chosen over division because
286: zero likelihoods make division lose recoverable histories.
287: 
288: This group is ready for independent substantive review when the full exchange,
289: fixtures and internal arithmetic/reference checks agree. Its finite capability
290: boundary is specified; continuous/mixed inference, causal identification,
291: calibration and empirical validation remain P17 obligations. Accounting, language,
292: persistence, interfaces, failure semantics, integrated profile/acceptance order,
293: E05 source conflict and full MC01–MC35/G1–G18 reconciliation remain open. Neither a
294: successful checker nor this candidate contract enables product implementation.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/uncertainty-admission-contract-v1.md
SHA256: 756bb611300b39e558e5f4274369e09135eb98b1e26531d8577ee6f7f9ad7a16
1: # Uncertainty admission and revision v1 — partial proposal
2: 
3: A company may have reported an amount without disclosing its composition. An
4: analyst may also be uncertain about what a note means, which accounting treatment
5: is permitted, or how management will respond to a future event. These questions
6: require different evidence and different ways of changing an answer. This
7: contract specifies those distinctions before attaching any probability model.
8: It addresses MC10/G15 and the MC04/MC08/MC12/MC14/MC18/MC25/MC27 interfaces.
9: The complete uncertainty contract still needs joint measures, conditioning,
10: probability updates and conditional future outcome payloads.
11: 
12: ## U1. Inputs, retained records and admission results
13: 
14: An `AdmissionRequest` contains an immutable proposed record, an operation,
15: a base-view version and a requested layer. Its shared header is:
16: 
17: | Field | Required meaning |
18: | --- | --- |
19: | `record_id`, `version`, `predecessor` | Stable logical identity, immutable version and explicit predecessor or null. Reusing an existing version with different content is invalid. A predecessor alone does not authorise supersession. |
20: | `category`, `payload_version` | One of the six categories below and the version of that category's payload rules. Unknown categories or payload versions remain preserved but unsupported. |
21: | `context` | Entity, perimeter/version, concept/mapping, dimensions, instant or reporting interval, calendar/version, currency, unit, scale, sign and measurement basis, with explicit unknown/not-applicable values. Numeric rounding follows E3; missing values never imply a global default. |
22: | `evidence_view`, `review_snapshot`, `time_view` | Exact immutable manifests; the time view includes admission mode, selection, cutoffs, calendars and byte-vintage evidence as required by T1–T3. |
23: | `scope` | Family ID/version, named branches or explicit all-branches selection, and candidate-selection key/version. Empty branch lists do not silently mean all. |
24: | `provenance` | Source occurrence/reading versions, derivations or authored assumptions, author, rationale and review-event references. Synthetic support is labelled synthetic. |
25: | `dependencies` | Exact typed/versioned dependencies, including applicability, information permissions, assumptions and selection membership. An empty list is explicit and still subject to category-required references. |
26: | `operation`, `requested_layer` | Operation from U3; evidence reading, reported-value, economic-history, accounting-history, decision-input or future-domain layer. Unsupported layer/category combinations return a reason. |
27: 
28: The category payload adds the substantive proposition or policy, not just a tag.
29: An operation event records its ID, author, rationale, proposed predecessor,
30: base view, selection changes and intended successor. Submitting an assertion
31: preserves it even when its interpretation or treatment cannot be admitted.
32: Invalid structured requests retain their submitted envelope and validation
33: errors for repair; they do not become valid semantic records.
34: 
35: An `AdmissionResult` contains request/record IDs, the old and proposed new view,
36: `disposition`, reason codes, requested layer, an admitted predicate or selected
37: assumption (null when neither is admitted), exact support references, unresolved
38: obligations, affected dependencies, family/branch effects, preserved record IDs,
39: and the next permissible operation. It keeps separate fields for:
40: 
41: * evidence review disposition and source truth (`not_established` unless a
42:   separately justified claim supplies more);
43: * category admission and conditional assumptions;
44: * accounting applicability/validity, which this admission alone cannot establish;
45: * capability support and computation status (these examples execute no engine).
46: 
47: `ADMITTED_CONSTRAINT` means the declared support and layer's admission rules
48: are satisfied, conditional on all listed assumptions. It does not make the
49: issuer's assertion true. `ADMITTED_ASSUMPTION` explicitly selects an authored
50: condition; `RETAINED_ONLY` leaves it unselected. `EVIDENCE_REFUTED` names a
51: supported predicate and a check that contradicts the candidate in this scope.
52: `UNRESOLVED` identifies missing information or an undecided check. `UNSUPPORTED`
53: identifies unavailable authority, semantics or capability. `REQUEST_INVALID`
54: identifies a malformed transition/reference. `SCOPED_CONFLICT` preserves an
55: incompatible proposed conjunction and its exact context. These dispositions
56: must not be collapsed into one Boolean or a probability.
57: 
58: ## U2. Six categories and their admission rules
59: 
60: ### Disclosure readings: extraction and semantic interpretation
61: 
62: An extraction payload links immutable bytes, occurrence/locator, literal reading
63: and extraction review. Review that accepts a reading establishes fidelity to the
64: inspected occurrence within that review's scope. A synthetic scan showing 120
65: refutes an OCR candidate 720 as a reading; it does not establish an underlying
66: company balance. A faithful reading of an erroneous issuer statement survives.
67: 
68: A semantic-interpretation payload separately names the extraction version,
69: contextual meaning, modality, mappings and alternatives. A phrase such as
70: “credit facility 100” does not establish that 100 is drawn debt. If commitment
71: and drawn-balance interpretations remain unresolved, retain both. Selecting one
72: as an explicit analytical assumption creates a conditional reported-value
73: branch; selecting an interpretation cannot manufacture authority for journals.
74: Review events must specify whether they concern fidelity or meaning. A semantic
75: review may admit a meaning at the requested layer only with its source/context
76: support; an unsupported implication remains unresolved. Competing readings
77: never receive weights from OCR confidence or reviewer preference. (UA01.)
78: 
79: ### Historical facts
80: 
81: A fact payload identifies an economic quantity/event, its declared domain and
82: supported constraints. Domains may be finite, continuous, mixed or symbolic;
83: any unsupported solver operation returns its unmodified domain as unresolved.
84: A continuous real-valued amount is not implicitly discretised to cents.
85: Evidence and context must support each new constraint, and shared quantities
86: retain their history-family links. A hypothetical assumption about an unknown
87: fact remains an assumption, even if it selects one factual value.
88: 
89: For the synthetic same-view amount x, let D0=[0,100]. New admissible evidence
90: supplies C1=[20,60]. Under unchanged assumptions, D1=D0 intersect C1=[20,60]:
91: x must satisfy both pairs of endpoint inequalities. Evidence C2=[70,80] gives
92: D1 intersect C2=empty since x<=60 and x>=70 cannot both hold. This is a conflict
93: in the named conjunction/branch, not a finding that every family or the real
94: company is impossible. A candidate x=80 is refuted within D1 because 80>60.
95: The closed endpoints, exact real domain and common units are explicit fixture
96: assumptions, not defaults for rounded reported values. (UA02.)
97: 
98: ### Accounting elections
99: 
100: An election payload names the dated rule package, exact permission and
101: eligibility evidence, relevant entity/transaction/class, effective period,
102: permitted options, chosen option and any consistency/transition obligations.
103: Permission must originate in the package's reviewed authority, not in a list
104: asserted by the request. Eligibility false refutes this proposed election within
105: that package; eligibility unknown remains unresolved. Missing or unverified
106: permission produces `UNSUPPORTED`, preserving the asserted treatment without
107: an accounting constraint. Even a selected permitted election needs separate
108: recognition/measurement/journal checks. An assumed election cannot be substituted
109: for evidence of what an issuer historically elected.
110: 
111: UA03 uses an explicitly synthetic rule P allowing options A and B during 2026
112: for class C when eligibility is true. Choosing B is a conditional design example;
113: option Z is refuted by P's exhaustive permission list. An absent real authority
114: is unsupported, not evidence that the treatment is prohibited in every regime.
115: No IFRS, US GAAP, ASBE or tax permission is established by this example. P17
116: must supply actual dated sources, eligibility and treatment consequences.
117: 
118: ### Measurement estimates
119: 
120: An estimate payload states the estimand, economic/measurement date, method and
121: version, units, inputs and their information dates, judgments, assumptions,
122: output domain/value and applicability requirements. A model-produced amount
123: remains an estimate. A reported estimate remains separately a reported assertion;
124: copying its number does not establish its measurement method.
125: 
126: UA04 uses the synthetic formula e=quantity times a stipulated price estimate:
127: 10 times 2=20. Later admissible input 3 produces a new estimate 30 for a newly
128: specified view of the same measurement target. The revised estimate is neither
129: a narrowed factual interval nor evidence that the earlier input was fraudulent.
130: Retrospective revision is labelled with the later information view; it cannot
131: be used by a decision confined to the original cutoff. Actual accounting rules
132: must determine whether a change is prospective, a correction, a restatement or
133: impermissible. Missing applicability is retained as an obligation. General
134: estimators are representable; unsupported evaluation returns the symbolic method,
135: inputs and reason without inventing a value. (UA04.)
136: 
137: ### Behaviour policies
138: 
139: A policy payload identifies its decision date(s), feasible actions/constraints,
140: state and information inputs, versioned decision rule, author and assumption
141: selection. A policy is a proposed conditional choice rule, not a realised fact.
142: All reads must resolve to the actor's declared information view at the decision
143: instant, including dynamically selected inputs and model-generated forecasts.
144: An audit list alone cannot prove that an eventual plugin obeys this rule.
145: Runtime information mediation is an interfaces/language obligation.
146: 
147: UA05's decision is dated 2026-03-01. An announcement known on 2026-02-28 about
148: a June stress can be used as announced information. The actual June outcome,
149: known in July, cannot be used at that decision date. Admission of the announcement
150: does not condition a historical probability model or make the June outcome known.
151: A fixed-action or state-dependent policy is permitted as a selected assumption
152: when its semantics and information checks are supported; the P06 hold-only
153: restriction is not a whole-product rule. Optimisation, randomised choices and
154: causal interventions need separately specified methods. Unsupported evaluation
155: preserves the policy but produces no executed decision. (UA05.)
156: 
157: ### Future innovations
158: 
159: A future-innovation payload identifies driver, future period, unit, scenario
160: version, feasible domain, joint constraints and assumptions. It may specify
161: unweighted possibilities. UA06 uses the synthetic continuous rate change
162: [-1/10,1/10], with no law. The future-domain layer can admit that authored domain
163: as a selected assumption without claiming any probability, independence,
164: expectation or relative plausibility. Correlated/shared driver constraints must
165: survive; the six category tags do not make variables independent.
166: 
167: A probability attachment, likelihood update, conditional distribution or measure
168: conversion requires the later joint-measure contract. For this slice these
169: operations return `UNSUPPORTED: JOINT_MEASURE_CONTRACT_REQUIRED`, preserving
170: the proposed model. No uniform distribution, equal branch weights, confidence
171: weights or normalization over successful branches is allowed. Absence of a law
172: is not grounds for rejecting an otherwise admissible unweighted scenario.
173: 
174: ## U3. Operations and revisions
175: 
176: Each operation names a base view and exact input versions. The support must pass
177: context, review, authority where applicable, and time checks for the requested
178: layer. A stale base view is `REQUEST_INVALID: STALE_BASE_VIEW`; retry requires
179: rebasing and reassessing newly competing evidence. No partial write may expose
180: new constraints with old dependency metadata. Persistence must atomically save
181: the new records, review/selection events, dependency changes and successor view;
182: its transaction/replay mechanism is still to be specified.
183: 
184: | Operation | Preconditions | Postconditions |
185: | --- | --- | --- |
186: | `new_evidence` | Resolved source/reading references, explicit context and view, category checks. New evidence can also be retained while checks are unresolved. | Preserve the source and proposal; admit only supported predicates at the requested layer. Recompute candidate membership even when the new candidate is not selected. A new source cannot silently replace the incumbent. |
187: | `narrow` | Same target, units, assumptions, interpretation/election choices and branch scope; new constraint has independent admissible support. | Child domain is the intersection of old domain and supported new constraint. Retain both supports. Empty intersection creates a scoped conflict; unknown intersection remains unresolved. Domain enlargement or changed assumptions is not narrowing. |
188: | `correct` / `supersede` | Exact predecessor, supported explanation of the error/correction and a review event naming which assertion/meaning/constraint is replaced and in which view. | New immutable version removes only the superseded active constraint in the successor view and supplies its replacement. No monotonic-domain claim. Original source and frozen views survive; other constraints still apply. A different economic target normally requires a new record, not replacement. |
189: | `select_assumption` | Authored proposition and alternatives, explicit rationale, scope and time view; requested layer permits conditional assumptions. | New conditional branch retains the original uncertainty and selected assumption. Unselected assumptions impose no constraint. Selection cannot establish factual truth or missing accounting permission. |
190: | `select_election` | Dated authority, eligibility and consistency/transition obligations established within scope; selection distinguishes assumed treatment from evidenced historical election. | New election version/conditional branch plus its obligations. Actual postings remain subject to accounting checks. Unsupported permission admits no treatment branch. |
191: | `revise_estimate` | Named old estimate, new method/input/judgment versions, same estimand or explicitly new target, valid information view and method applicability. | New estimate and lineage, invalidated dependent measurements/results, frozen prior estimate. Record how information date differs from measurement date; accounting treatment of the revision remains separately determined. |
192: | `revise_policy` / `revise_driver` | Named predecessor, explicit changed rule/domain/information set/scenario and rationale, capability and information checks. | New selected assumption/version. Reassess all affected paths and membership; no automatic narrowing, causal effect or change in historical weights. Old paths remain frozen. |
193: 
194: For a supported correction of [20,60] to [10,90], keeping background [0,100],
195: the new domain is [0,100] intersect [10,90]=[10,90]. Intersecting with the
196: superseded [20,60] would retain a known obsolete restriction. This example
197: stipulates that the correction replaces that entire bound and that no other
198: constraint survives; actual mixed-support claims must identify precisely which
199: part is corrected. A frozen prior query still returns [20,60]. (UA07a.)
200: 
201: Invalidation uses reverse dependencies, not only selected-record IDs. If the
202: old reading feeds interpretation I, fact constraint C, family F and query Q,
203: changing the reading invalidates I, C, F and Q for *current* reuse. If an estimate
204: E also uses C, its result is affected as well. Adding a rival source changes the
205: selection-population revision even if the winning candidate stays identical;
206: F and Q depend on that revision. Missing dependency metadata makes current reuse
207: unestablished. Unrelated families may be reused only with complete unchanged
208: dependencies and matching query/time views. Invalidation changes reuse status,
209: not the bytes of old results, and is not evidence that every old number changed.
210: A frozen export identifies its original version, assumptions and supersession
211: status; it never advertises an old result as current. (UA07b.)
212: 
213: The provenance/derivation graph must resolve all references and must not contain
214: unsupported cycles. A reference to a version that does not exist is a dangling
215: reference, even if a record with the same logical name exists. Cyclic economic
216: relations are a different matter: x+y=100 can jointly constrain both variables
217: in one supported constraint block without either being independently known.
218: Mutually constrained quantities do not imply a cyclic derivation or independent
219: random variables. A simultaneous block names its formula, variables and external
220: support; an unsupported block is retained as unresolved. Category substitution
221: (e.g. replacing an unknown fact with an election under the same identity) is
222: invalid. A legitimate cross-category inference creates a distinct record with
223: explicit derivation and category-specific admission. (UA08.)
224: 
225: ## U4. Interactions, failures and next operations
226: 
227: | Contract | Exchange and responsibility |
228: | --- | --- |
229: | Evidence E1/E3/E4 | Supplies occurrences, extraction and interpretation review, context, conflicts and assumptions. Admission returns the predicate/assumption actually used, its scope and unresolved supports. Retention is independent of admission; no source text is overwritten. |
230: | Time T1–T3 | Supplies cutoff-qualified evidence and byte-vintage/review views. Admission checks information access at measurement and decision dates, and records later-information revisions separately. Unknown availability cannot pass a historical-information requirement. |
231: | Histories H1/H2/H5 | Consumes jointly admitted predicates, selected assumptions and membership revisions. Returns conditional families/candidate regions and unresolved obligations. Partial support never validates membership in the complete accounting-history family. |
232: | Accounting | Must establish package authority, eligibility, recognition, measurement and revision consequences. Receives retained unsupported treatments separately from selected permitted elections; returns layer-specific applicability and journal obligations. |
233: | Language/IR | Must encode category payloads, exact domains/units, simultaneous blocks, dependency types and explicit operation transitions. Unknown versions cannot be decoded using a convenient existing variant. These JSON examples do not select a runtime format/library. |
234: | Persistence | Owns atomic version/view creation, idempotency, stale-write detection, migration and reverse dependencies. Frozen reads must be distinguishable from current reuse; a failed write leaves the old view current with an explicit failure receipt. |
235: | Interfaces and failure semantics | Show requested layer, retained content, disposition, conditions, time view, unresolved obligations and next action. Preserve partial results on capability/resource failure. Harmonize reason codes without converting unsupported/unknown into false or successful computation. |
236: 
237: A refuted candidate can be reconsidered under a supported correction or an
238: explicitly different conditional view; its refutation remains valid in the old
239: scope. An unsupported treatment needs authority/applicability work. A dangling
240: reference needs correction, not solver execution. A local conflict needs source,
241: context or interpretation review; arbitrary deletion of the inconvenient source
242: is not a repair. Solver timeout preserves the expression and unresolved region.
243: It cannot establish evidence refutation or global family infeasibility.
244: 
245: Full scenario-mode semantics, stochastic information filtrations, joint measures,
246: likelihood identity/reuse, probability normalization and conditional outcome
247: payloads are the next uncertainty task. No operation in U3 changes probability
248: weights. A future implementation that attaches a model must identify that later
249: contract and preserve unavailable outcome regions rather than renormalizing
250: successful computations. The proposed interface here is a dependency obligation,
251: not a claim that these semantics are already complete.
252: 
253: ## U5. Matched encoding alternatives and default audit
254: 
255: Both alternatives receive the same UA01–UA08 requests and must return the same
256: expected dispositions, retained records, family effects and invalidations.
257: Neither representation is credited for accepting more unsupported cases.
258: 
259: | Same fixture question | Tagged variants with shared header | Generic record with versioned validator registry |
260: | --- | --- | --- |
261: | UA01: fidelity versus meaning | Two explicit payload variants within disclosure readings require different fields/reviews. | A category/subtype validator must distinguish them; generic fields alone cannot. |
262: | UA02: continuous domains | Domain variant carries exact endpoints/symbolic predicates; no finite-only restriction. | Domain-validator version must be pinned; changing it cannot silently change endpoint meaning. |
263: | UA03: permission versus asserted choice | Election variant requires authority and eligibility references; absence returns unsupported. | Registry validator must enforce the same sourced permissions, not trust arbitrary fields. |
264: | UA04: estimate/date revision | Estimate transition checks input versions and view separately from fact narrowing. | Operation and category validators must be coordinated and versioned together. |
265: | UA05: future information | Policy variant exposes decision inputs and their information roles. | Extensible policy validator must still use the shared information rule; extensions cannot bypass it. |
266: | UA06: unweighted future domain | Law attachment is explicitly optional; this slice rejects probability operations. | Registry must separate domain admission from a later probability plugin and report its absence. |
267: | UA07: corrections and invalidation | Shared revision header and typed dependency edges support the declared successor/frozen distinction. | Shared validator protocol must supply all changed dependencies; an extension that omits them cannot support reuse. |
268: | UA08: substitutions and cycles | Closed category discriminants reject wrong payload/identity changes; simultaneous blocks have their own representation. | Unknown/new categories are easier to register but must return unsupported until reviewed migration/admission semantics exist. |
269: 
270: Provisionally prefer tagged payload variants with the shared header for the
271: initial specification: the six categories have materially different admission
272: conditions, and explicit variants make missing conditions visible to reviewers.
273: A versioned registry remains credible where later domains need additional
274: payload types. The choice does not select Python classes, JSON as a wire format,
275: a database or a solver. Either needs an extension/migration policy preserving
276: old record interpretation, adapter conformance and unknown-version retention.
277: Changing category meaning requires a new version and explicit migration, never
278: reinterpretation of frozen content. This is a semantic comparison, not an
279: implemented benchmark or performance ranking.
280: 
281: | Material choice | Provenance and justification | Failure mode | Smallest diagnostic | Status |
282: | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
283: | Six categories, explicit semantic subtype | MC10/P15 G15 and E1; different admission rules | Treat extraction fidelity as factual truth | UA01 competing reading/meaning | User requirement; encoding proposed |
284: | Full shared context/view/header | E3/T1/H1/H5 and MC25/27 | Mix periods, views or source with assumptions | Inspect every fixture's context and layer | Proposed integration |
285: | Immutable versions and reverse membership dependencies | H5 and MC27 | Old query reused after a new rival source | UA07 closure and frozen read | Proposed behavior; transaction mechanism open |
286: | Intersect only for same-assumption narrowing | Set definition in U2/U3 | Widening called narrowing or correction overconstrained | UA02/UA07 exact endpoints | Derived semantics in declared example |
287: | Exact closed real intervals | Synthetic exercise, to expose continuous uncertainty | Accidentally infer cents or reported-rounding behavior | Check interior 1/3 in [0,100] and stated endpoints | Fixture convenience only |
288: | Synthetic P and multiply estimator | Bounded interface examples, no new authority research | Mistake permission or formula for accounting law | Check synthetic labels and pending accounting obligations | Convenience, never production default |
289: | Tagged variants | Matched U5 comparison | Hard-to-extend closed enumeration or concealed custom fields | UA08 unknown version and future migration review | Provisional proposal |
290: | Unweighted scenarios and no implicit independence | MC08/12/14/18 | Invent probabilities from enumeration/confidence | UA06 absent-law outcomes and deferred attachment | Required preservation; measure design unfinished |
291: | P06 finite facts, finite weighted innovations, hold-only policy and restricted methods | Historical `admit_uncertainty` at lines 539–686 | Promote probe limits into universal requirements | Compare UA02, UA05 and UA06 with those branches | Historical baseline only; not adopted |
292: 
293: ## U6. Scope of completion and source support
294: 
295: The companion fixture file specifies eight groups with explicit inputs, support,
296: expected results and preserved records. Its sources are synthetic by construction.
297: Local diagnostics check document/reference consistency, example arithmetic and
298: dependency closure. They cannot establish that a future implementation enforces
299: the rules, that source-grounded treatments are valid, or that forecasting works.
300: Independent substantive review and human acceptance remain pending.
301: 
302: The requirements come from `docs/plans/accounting-mission-requirements.md`
303: (MC04/08/10/12/14/18/25/27) and the accepted P15 `coverage.json` rows MC10/12/14
304: and G15. Interactions use proposed evidence-contract-v3 E1/E3/E4,
305: time-contract-v2 T1–T3 and history-families-contract-v1 H1/H2/H5. The inspected
306: P06 implementation is historical counterevidence for accidental defaults, not
307: accounting authority. No new academic theorem or official accounting permission
308: is asserted; the two interval relations are derived in U2/U3 and the estimate
309: multiplications are stipulated arithmetic.
310: 
311: Completion of this admission slice leaves the whole uncertainty contract
312: unfinished. The next separately reviewed task must specify justified joint
313: measures, conditioning, probability updates and conditional outcome payloads,
314: then reconcile both groups before registering uncertainty as a complete proposed
315: contract. Accounting, language/IR, persistence, interfaces and failure semantics,
316: initial-profile selection and dependency order also remain open. All fourteen
317: P15 decisions and MC01–MC35/G1–G18 remain active. Optional bounded result review
318: must not become a prerequisite that blocks the next reviewed drafting task;
319: full independent P16 result review remains required before phase acceptance.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/uncertainty-measure-attachment-contract-v2.md
SHA256: 3812e0f1f28f8bd77fbbd38f1b9b79810346a42ed47a42532b95d9aeb4a5a0b3
1: # Joint-measure attachment and event identity v2
2: 
3: This document specifies the next bounded part of the P16 uncertainty contract.
4: It attaches a declared joint measure to an already versioned history family and
5: evaluates events against that one measure. It does not select a measure from
6: data, infer independence, condition on observations, update likelihoods, run a
7: solver, or produce financial statements. The examples are synthetic design
8: fixtures; they establish no probability for an actual company and no
9: accounting permission.
10: 
11: This version repairs the saved examples only. Systematic matched-alternative
12: assessment and remaining failure-case coverage remain the next bounded task.
13: 
14: The contract is needed for MC08--MC14, MC18, MC25 and MC27 and for G1, G15 and
15: G16. It consumes the evidence, time, history-family and admission records
16: already described in this directory. Accounting validity, empirical forecast
17: validity, implementation correctness and human acceptance remain separate
18: questions.
19: 
20: ## A. Request, result and identity
21: 
22: `MeasureAttachmentRequest` is an immutable, versioned record with these fields:
23: 
24: | Field | Required meaning |
25: | --- | --- |
26: | `request_id`, `version`, `predecessor` | Stable request identity and an explicit predecessor; changing the bytes creates a new version. |
27: | `family_ref`, `view_ref` | Exact immutable history-family and view references, each resolving an ID and version. A measure never silently follows a successor family. |
28: | `model_ref` | Exact model ID/version reference with the declared measure representation and evaluator. Explicit null preserves an unweighted domain. |
29: | `coordinates` | Ordered typed coordinates, including entity/perimeter, concept, dimensions, units, time and any shared drivers. Coordinate identity is semantic, not an array position chosen by a caller. |
30: | `sample_space` | Explicit elementary outcomes or a declared continuous/mixed domain, with support and measurability metadata. Missing support is unresolved. |
31: | `event_representation` | Versioned selectors for elementary outcomes, measurable cells or symbolic predicates. A display label is never an event key. |
32: | `measure_kind`, `purpose` | `finite_joint`, `discrete_joint`, `continuous_joint` or `mixed_joint`; purpose is one of scenario, pricing, risk, or another declared use. Purposes cannot be mixed implicitly. |
33: | `mass_or_density` | Exact masses, a versioned density, or a symbolic measure expression. Finite examples use rational strings; unsupported evaluation retains the expression. |
34: | `support_restrictions`, `dependence` | Restrictions and all declared cross-coordinate dependence. Empty dependence means “unspecified”, not independence. |
35: | `provenance`, `justification` | Source/model record versions, author, rationale, assumptions and whether the law is synthetic, stipulated, estimated or empirically supported. |
36: | `information_cutoff`, `evidence_cutoff` | Time views used to justify the model and to admit its inputs. Later information cannot silently enter an earlier view. |
37: | `dependencies` | Exact versioned family, evidence, time, category and model dependencies. A missing or stale dependency blocks current reuse. |
38: 
39: Section F defines the exact field names and operation-specific result schema.
40: The result is a `MeasureAttachmentResult` with the request and record identity,
41: old and proposed view, a disposition, reason codes, preserved records,
42: support references, unresolved obligations, structural validity, attachment
43: status, mathematical obligations, empirical-justification status,
44: capability status, computation status and permitted next operations. These
45: fields are separate. A syntactically valid finite law can be attached as an
46: assumption while its empirical justification remains `not_established`.
47: 
48: The dispositions are:
49: 
50: * `ATTACHED_ASSUMPTION`: the declared law is structurally valid and explicitly
51:   admitted as a model assumption; no empirical claim follows.
52: * `ATTACHED_SUPPORTED`: a separately inspected source/model record supports the
53:   law for this purpose and scope. This status is not created by a fixture.
54: * `RETAINED_ONLY`: the submission is preserved but not selected.
55: * `REQUEST_INVALID`: identity, version, support, representation or the proposed
56:   finite probability law fails its stated invariant.
57: * `UNSUPPORTED`: the requested evaluator or measure representation is outside
58:   declared capability; the expression and domain remain intact.
59: * `SCOPED_CONFLICT`: restrictions or attached records are incompatible in the
60:   named family/view. It is not a claim of global infeasibility.
61: 
62: ## B. Attachment invariants
63: 
64: 1. The family/view, model version, coordinate order, purpose and information
65:    cutoffs are part of the identity. A law attached to `F@1/V@1` cannot be
66:    reused for `F@2/V@1` merely because the labels look alike. A changed support
67:    invalidates reuse until an explicit alignment or update operation records
68:    what changed.
69: 2. For a finite joint law, each elementary outcome ID is unique, each mass is
70:    finite and non-negative, and the exact total is one. A duplicate ID is a
71:    malformed representation, not extra mass. The declared sample space S is
72:    distinct from positive-mass support {s in S: m(s)>0}. Every member of a finite
73:    S has an explicit mass, including zero. Omitting a member is an error, not an
74:    assertion of zero mass. An event may include zero-mass members. A partition
75:    must cover S, not merely its positive-mass subset.
76: 3. No mass is inferred from solver counts, branch counts, labels, extraction
77:    confidence, reviewer preference, successful-run frequency or a convenient
78:    equal-weight default. An unweighted future domain remains unweighted until a
79:    separate attachment is explicitly accepted.
80: 4. Dependence is joint information. Identical marginal summaries do not permit
81:    an independence assumption. Shared drivers, entity links, dates, lots and
82:    contract states stay in the coordinate and dependency records.
83: 5. A real-world scenario measure and a pricing or risk measure are different
84:    records. Changing `purpose` requires a new attachment and justification; it
85:    is not a unit conversion.
86: 6. An attachment may be conditional on admitted evidence, an authored
87:    assumption, or a future-domain restriction. It cannot turn an unsupported
88:    accounting election into authority or turn a reported value into an economic
89:    fact.
90: 7. Every accepted operation is append-only. A correction creates a new measure
91:    version and records the predecessor, changed support and affected results.
92:    Old views remain readable as historical records but are not current by
93:    default. Failed writes leave the old view current and return a failure
94:    receipt.
95: 
96: The historical Core 0 probe required a joint law for future innovations and
97: therefore rejected an unweighted future domain. This proposal supersedes that
98: probe restriction for the whole product: an unweighted domain is admissible as
99: an explicit scenario assumption, but probability, expectation, likelihood and
100: quantile operations require this attachment contract and an attached law. The
101: older restriction remains evidence about the prototype, not a universal
102: semantic rule.
103: 
104: ## C. Event identity and evaluation
105: 
106: An `EventIdentity` contains an event ID/version, the exact family/view and
107: measure identity, coordinate bindings, selector kind, selector expression,
108: projection metadata and provenance. A named event may be an overlapping view;
109: it need not be a cell in a partition. Distinct elementary outcomes that map to
110: the same displayed coordinate remain distinct IDs. A duplicate event
111: description with the same identity is an idempotent reference; a new selector
112: version is a new event.
113: 
114: For a finite space `S` with masses `m(s)`, a selector `E` evaluates as
115: 
116: \[
117:   P(E)=\sum_{s\in S:\,E(s)}m(s).
118: \]
119: 
120: For events `A` and `B`, evaluation uses the same joint law:
121: 
122: \[
123: P(A\cap B)=\sum_{s:\,A(s)\land B(s)}m(s),\qquad
124: P(A\cup B)=P(A)+P(B)-P(A\cap B).
125: \]
126: 
127: The subtraction is required when views overlap; summing named-view masses
128: without the intersection double-counts shared outcomes. A finite partition must
129: carry coverage and pairwise-disjointness evidence before a caller may treat its
130: cell masses as an exhaustive decomposition. For continuous or mixed spaces,
131: the request preserves the declared domain and measure expression. If no
132: validated evaluator is available, the result is `UNSUPPORTED` with the
133: symbolic event and unresolved numerical obligation; it never fabricates a
134: grid, density normalization or probability.
135: 
136: Projection is explicit. If two elementary outcomes `(region=A, rate=1)` and
137: `(region=B, rate=1)` both project to `rate=1`, the projected display has mass
138: one but the elementary outcomes remain two distinct members of the joint
139: space. Collapsing them is allowed only through a named measurable projection
140: that records the lost coordinate and the resulting event algebra.
141: 
142: ## D. Failures and interactions
143: 
144: The evidence and admission contracts supply immutable records, category
145: dispositions and selected assumptions. The time contract supplies information
146: cutoffs, byte vintages and review views. Histories consume an attached law and
147: return family-scoped events; they must retain unweighted domains and unresolved
148: branches when no law is attached. Accounting receives a measure only as a
149: scenario/model input and must perform its own recognition, measurement and
150: journal checks. Language/IR must encode exact versions, selectors, domains and
151: reason codes. Persistence owns atomic append, stale-version detection and
152: reverse dependencies. Interfaces expose the purpose, conditions, cutoffs,
153: support, dependence and unresolved obligations. Failure semantics preserve
154: partial records on resource or evaluator failure.
155: 
156: The following are explicit failures: negative or non-unit finite mass;
157: duplicate elementary IDs; omitted support; stale family/model version;
158: incompatible purpose; implicit independence; event references outside the
159: declared space; a partition advertised without coverage/disjointness evidence;
160: and unsupported continuous evaluation. None of these failures authorises
161: discarding mass, renormalising successful branches, or replacing an unresolved
162: outcome with a default.
163: 
164: Conditioning, likelihood updates, measure conversion, calibration and
165: conditional statement/outcome payloads are future operations. They must name a
166: denominator/event identity, information set and resulting family version and
167: will be specified in a separate reviewed task.
168: 
169: ## E. Matched alternatives and default audit
170: 
171: Two credible representations are proposed for a future matched assessment
172: against the same fixtures. That assessment has not been completed:
173: 
174: * **Family-linked record (provisional choice).** The attachment stores the exact
175:   family/view and references the model record. Revisions and invalidation are
176:   straightforward, and a reader can trace a probability back to the joint
177:   history family. Portability requires a stable family identity service.
178: * **Self-contained joint-model record.** The attachment carries a complete
179:   joint model and an explicit projection to a family. It is easier to export,
180:   but duplicated family definitions can drift and a correction must reconcile
181:   two version graphs.
182: 
183: The family-linked design remains a provisional candidate because preserving
184: shared history identity and revision lineage is a stated mission requirement.
185: This is a design rationale, not a performance or usability ranking; both
186: alternatives remain implementation candidates until the language, persistence
187: and interface contracts are reviewed. The material defaults have the following
188: audit:
189: 
190: | Choice | Provenance and justification | Failure mode | Smallest diagnostic | Status |
191: | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
192: | Rational finite masses in fixtures | Exact arithmetic makes overlap and normalization inspectable. | Decimal parsing could hide rounding. | Recompute with `Fraction` and compare exact strings. | Synthetic diagnostic convention |
193: | Explicit elementary IDs | Needed to distinguish equal projections. | Callers may use labels as IDs. | Duplicate-ID and projection-collision fixtures. | Proposed invariant |
194: | No implicit independence | Required by joint-history dependence. | Marginal-only input may be treated as independent. | Same-marginal/different-joint fixtures. | Proposed invariant |
195: | Separate purpose | Scenario and pricing measures answer different questions. | A converter may relabel a measure. | Purpose-mismatch rejection fixture. | Proposed invariant |
196: | Family-linked identity | Preserves revisions and dependency closure. | Export/import may lack family registry. | Version-mismatch and changed-support fixtures. | Candidate; profile unselected |
197: 
198: All fixture outputs are specified behavior only. They do not show that a
199: runtime, accounting treatment, company probability or forecast is correct.
200: 
201: 
202: ## F. Operation-specific records and fixture convention (v2)
203: 
204: The companion v2 JSON stores complete request/result records in each case. There
205: are no implicit case overrides or defaults. `records` is an immutable registry:
206: its key equals `id@version`, and references always use that complete key.
207: `family_ref`, `view_ref`, `model_ref`, coordinate references and dependency lists
208: resolve there. An explicitly null model denotes an unweighted domain, not a
209: missing field. A stored malformed model remains a submitted candidate; registry
210: presence is not admission. The same key must never describe different bytes.
211: Family and view versions are independent; a view identifies exactly one family
212: version. Model versions bind the family, view, coordinate order, sample space,
213: purpose, dependence and mass/density representation. A new law requires a new
214: key. These examples use explicit references rather than duplicating id/version
215: pairs that could disagree.
216: 
217: Every request has `request_id`, `version`, `predecessor`, `operation`, exact
218: family/view/model references, ordered `coordinates`, `sample_space`,
219: `measure_kind`, `purpose`, `mass_or_density`, `dependence`,
220: `support_restrictions`, `event_representation`, `provenance`, `justification`,
221: `information_cutoff`, `evidence_cutoff`, and `dependencies`. Coordinates resolve
222: to typed concept/entity/perimeter/dimension/unit/time metadata. Dependencies
223: include every direct semantic reference and the time/admission/evidence records.
224: Transitive dependencies follow the registry references. Cutoffs here are both
225: 2026-03-01T00:00:00Z; fixture records were authored for this synthetic time view.
226: No claim about the information available for a real company follows.
227: 
228: `MeasureAttachmentRequest` uses `operation=attach_measure`. The deliberately
229: invalid branch-count request uses `infer_from_branch_count`; its rejection is
230: specified even though this is not an admitted operation. Marginal-only inputs
231: are retained as constraints on possible laws, with no attached joint measure.
232: For finite laws, masses cover the declared outcomes exactly and joint outcome
233: assignments cover every coordinate. `support_restrictions=[]` means no added
234: restriction, not permission to discard an outcome. A density is expressed
235: relative to its declared base measure; missing normalization remains explicit.
236: 
237: `EventEvaluationRequest` uses `operation=evaluate_event` and also requires
238: `attachment_ref`, `event_refs`, and `query`. The attachment is either an admitted
239: synthetic precondition or a retained symbolic candidate with explicit status.
240: Each event binds its own model/family/view and coordinates, selector kind,
241: selector expression, projection metadata and provenance. Queries are one-event
242: probability, two-event overlap, finite partition validation, or finite
243: projection. MA03c repeats one event reference; it does not request an evidence
244: update and proves nothing about semantic duplicate likelihoods. Symbolic
245: requests retain expressions when the proposed evaluator cannot compute them.
246: 
247: These examples validate required fields and resolvable references first, then
248: attachment family/support, purpose and model compatibility. An incompatible
249: family/purpose is rejected; a newer requested model is retained without reuse.
250: For finite inputs, duplicate IDs precede mass coverage, non-negativity and unit
251: total checks. Event selection/partition checks follow valid-law checks. Symbolic
252: models retain missing mathematical obligations when evaluation is unsupported.
253: An invalid probability law can have a well-formed representation: MA02b/c thus
254: have valid structure and failed mathematics. A request without a joint law has
255: unresolved mathematics, not an assertion that no such law exists.
256: 
257: Every result includes `result_id`, `version`, `request_ref`, `operation`,
258: `old_view`, `proposed_view`, `disposition`, `reason_codes`, `preserved_records`,
259: `support_references`, `unresolved_obligations`, `structural_validity`,
260: `attachment_status`, `mathematical_status`, `mathematical_obligations`,
261: `empirical_justification`, `capability_status`, `computation_status`,
262: `next_permissible_operations`, `future_dependencies`, `family_effect`,
263: `probability`, and `observations`. Attachment success additionally identifies
264: `attachment_ref`; evaluated results retain the input attachment. `probability`
265: is populated only for a single defined event; multi-event/projection answers
266: are named in `observations`. Invalid total mass is an observation about a
267: candidate, not an event probability. Unsupported or rejected evaluations return
268: no numerical probability. Receipt preservation is not an attached law.
269: 
270: Attachment dispositions retain section A meanings. Event results add
271: `EVALUATED` for a computed, mathematically valid query under the named law;
272: `REQUEST_INVALID` for incompatible identity/purpose or malformed selectors;
273: `RETAINED_ONLY` for a stale model that needs explicit migration; and
274: `UNSUPPORTED` for a retained symbolic request without a validated evaluator.
275: `structural_validity` concerns the representation, not normalization;
276: `mathematical_status` is independently `satisfied`, `failed`, or `unresolved`.
277: An unavailable evaluator can leave structure valid and mathematics unresolved.
278: All empirical statuses here are `not_established`. `capability_status` means
279: capability proposed by this document, never an installed runtime capability.
280: All results are specified expectations, not executed product responses.
281: 
282: An attachment appends a measure record referencing its existing family/view;
283: it does not change the history family or select a probability for unweighted
284: alternatives. `old_view` and `proposed_view` therefore match in these examples.
285: Rejections leave the old view/attachment current. Stale-model and changed-family
286: requests preserve that original view and require a new explicitly reviewed
287: attachment; they cannot silently redirect it. Atomic persistence remains a
288: separate unfinished contract.
289: 
290: The currently specified next operations are revise_request, attach_measure,
291: evaluate_event, and retain_symbolic. These names describe the proposal only.
292: Conditioning, likelihood updates, model migration, evaluator installation and
293: conditional financial outcomes occur only in `future_dependencies`. No example
294: advertises condition_or_update as a presently permissible operation.
295: 
296: ## G. Repaired examples and exact local deductions
297: 
298: MA01 preserves an unweighted continuous domain and rejects branch counting as
299: a law. MA02 uses one three-atom family and separately identified valid,
300: negative-mass, non-unit and duplicate-ID submissions. MA03 evaluates the valid
301: law with masses 1/2, 1/3, 1/6: for A={a,b} and B={b,c}, P(A)=5/6,
302: P(B)=1/2, P(A intersection B)=1/3, hence P(A union B)=5/6+1/2-1/3=1.
303: A partition omitting c fails even though its listed cells are disjoint.
304: 
305: MA04a retains Bernoulli marginals without inventing a coupling. MA04c and MA04d
306: use the same four assignments (0,0), (0,1), (1,0), (1,1). The first law places
307: 1/2 on each diagonal assignment and explicit zeros elsewhere; the second puts
308: 1/2 on each off-diagonal assignment and zeros on the diagonal. Summing over Y
309: or X gives the same Bernoulli(1/2) marginals under each law. Summing the diagonal
310: masses gives P(X=Y)=1 and 0. Thus these marginals cannot determine that event.
311: These are local finite-sum deductions, not claims from an external source.
312: 
313: MA04b has its own two-outcome family and law: (A,1) and (B,1), each mass 1/2.
314: The explicit rate projection sums both preimages to mass 1 while preserving
315: both elementary IDs. This law never reuses the three-atom model identity.
316: MA05 rejects changed family/support and changed purpose, and retains a stale
317: model request without migrating it. MA06 uses distinct compatible continuous
318: and mixed domains/models. Both preserve their symbolic expressions and missing
319: mathematical/evaluator obligations, with null numerical probability.
320: 
321: The finite fixture convention is exact rational arithmetic, not a proposed
322: restriction on all real implementations. The local diagnostic recomputes from
323: inputs, checks identity/reference/domain compatibility, and compares specified
324: outputs. It is an author consistency check, not an engine or prose acceptance
325: test. Comprehensive failure coverage, matched alternatives and uncertainty
326: integration remain open; independent substantive review is pending.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/uncertainty-chaining-contract-v1.md
SHA256: 58be776f6587bf83b34157b8f9e8041189654bd47028134b40582c5087214d78
1: # Uncertainty operation chaining — proposed supplement v1
2: 
3: This supplement repairs the result-to-request boundary in uncertainty-contract-v1,
4: sections 2–4, and the UC01, UC02 and UC08 fixtures. Those immutable fixtures
5: contain correct illustrative masses but their child `model_ref` points to the
6: original prior. That reference is wrong as the governing law for a subsequent
7: query. The mappings in uncertainty-chaining-fixtures-v1.json replace that meaning
8: for this proposal; the original records remain historical evidence. No product
9: implementation or independent acceptance is claimed.
10: 
11: ## 1. Bounded question and evidence contract
12: 
13: Can a specified uncertainty result become the next request without reverting to
14: prior masses, losing consumed information or silently granting admission? The
15: comparator is attachment v2 A–F and uncertainty admission U3: exact immutable
16: bindings, explicit selection and preserved parents. This task addresses MC12,
17: MC14, MC25, MC27, MC33–MC35, with MC08/G6/G15 interactions. It does not select
18: the initial profile, accounting policy, persistence engine or inference method.
19: 
20: Pass requires consistent complete bundles, an explicit admission boundary, exact
21: finite chained arithmetic and rejection receipts preserving domain state.
22: A mismatched law/view/attachment, repeated information or broken reference vetoes
23: internal consistency and requires repair. Structural checker counts explain
24: coverage only. There is no empirical comparison, stochastic ranking, accounting
25: eligibility finding or runtime transaction claim. Stop after these specifications
26: and bounded checks; independent uncertainty review is next, before accounting.
27: 
28: | Choice and provenance | Rationale and failure mode | Early diagnostic; status |
29: | --- | --- | --- |
30: | Immutable derived law, attachment B | Separate provenance from the law actually used; otherwise later queries use prior masses. | Consume UC02's output and reject an original-law substitution; provisional representation. |
31: | Separate admission/selection, uncertainty section 2 and admission U3 | A calculated candidate is not permission to use it in an admitted analysis. | Candidate-only request fails; admission receipt names exact bundle; proposed invariant. |
32: | Full finite space, attachment B.2 | A zero-mass outcome remains identifiable for future correction. | Retain c at zero through ordinary updates and restore it only by rebase; finite example convenience. |
33: | Stipulated binary observation kernel, uncertainty section 4 | Conditional factors require a joint model; distinct atoms alone do not justify independence. | Recompute sequential and joint posteriors; hypothetical model, no calibration claim. |
34: 
35: Skeptical audit passed for this bounded document repair: fixed semantic baseline,
36: explicit stop, no proxy promotion, no runtime/environment comparison and no
37: transfer of prototype defaults. Both representation alternatives below must
38: answer the same chained question. Five counterpart contracts, all fourteen open
39: decisions, the integrated initial profile and all wider mission obligations remain.
40: 
41: ## 2. Complete derivation bundle
42: 
43: A successful weighted condition/update emits an immutable bundle containing:
44: 
45: * a child view with exact family, coordinate, time and evidence references,
46:   parent view, operation request, `model_ref` for its governing derived law,
47:   candidate attachment, consumed atoms and assumption-condition identities;
48: * a derived law with the same child view/family/ordered coordinates/purpose,
49:   full sample space, every mass including zero, parent law and `base_model_ref`;
50: * a candidate attachment binding that child and law, with status
51:   `candidate_not_admitted`, plus the input admission used to justify derivation;
52: * operation lineage naming parent, condition or likelihood/observation records,
53:   normalizer, unnormalized masses, incremental/conditioning atoms and applicable
54:   corrected/superseded records; and
55: * a result receipt naming all created records, payload and separate structural,
56:   mathematical, empirical, capability, accounting and human-acceptance statuses.
57: 
58: `base_model_ref` is origin/provenance, never an instruction to evaluate with the
59: base law. `parent_model_ref` identifies the immediate mathematical input;
60: `model_ref` identifies the law governing the child. A request's view, law and
61: attachment must agree on exact identities, not merely numerical masses. The
62: view's displayed masses, receipt posterior and governing-law masses must agree.
63: All typed references resolve; dependencies include their transitive closure.
64: Mutual view/law references are identity links, not recursive content hashes;
65: each registry record is hashed as canonical JSON separately.
66: 
67: For P on S and nonnegative factor f, the lineage stores w(s)=P(s)f(s),
68: Z=sum w(s)>0 and Q(s)=w(s)/Z. A condition uses f=1_A. Zero Z produces no bundle.
69: Consumed observations are the union of parent consumed atoms and the new
70: incremental atoms. Assumption conditions have separate semantic identities;
71: conditioning on an authored scenario does not itself consume a measurement.
72: If a condition represents observed information, its atom must instead be recorded
73: and protected against later likelihood reuse. Calibration atoms of the base law
74: remain in the information audit even if absent from the view's incremental list.
75: 
76: Output creation never changes the selected pointer. The receipt's domain writes
77: are exactly the new view/law/candidate attachment/lineage records; a stored receipt
78: is an audit write. Candidate presence alone never grants admission. Unsupported
79: continuous/mixed operations keep their symbolic obligations and do not manufacture
80: finite bundles. Unweighted results continue under the earlier feasible-set rules.
81: 
82: ## 3. Admission and the next request
83: 
84: `admit_derived_bundle` takes the exact candidate/result, intended time/evidence
85: view and purpose, requested assumption scope, named reviewer/decision authority,
86: rationale and the expected selection revision. It verifies bundle closure,
87: normalization, binding, input admissibility, information lineage and complete
88: support. It returns an immutable admission decision, an admitted attachment
89: binding the same child/law, and a receipt. The candidate attachment is retained.
90: Neither law nor view is mutated to change its status. For these synthetic
91: fixtures the decision authority is the fixture author and the only admissible
92: status is `admitted_as_authored_assumption`; this is not accounting, empirical or
93: independent approval. Real authority and review policy remain caller obligations.
94: 
95: A separate `select_admitted_view` names that receipt and attachment, the expected
96: current selection revision and rationale. It returns a new selection revision
97: and receipt. Admission alone leaves the selected pointer unchanged. Explicit
98: queries may name any still-admissible frozen view without changing selection;
99: requests for the current view must match the current revision. Corrections record
100: supersession/reuse status in new records, preserving frozen historical queries.
101: A concurrent/stale selection revision rejects with `STALE_BASE_VIEW`.
102: 
103: The next probability request must name the child's governing law, admitted
104: attachment and admission receipt, with matching purpose/time/evidence scope.
105: A candidate-only attachment fails `ADMISSION_REQUIRED`; old prior-law or
106: parent-attachment substitutions fail `LAW_VIEW_ATTACHMENT_MISMATCH` before
107: arithmetic. No fallback to an earlier admission or newest record is permitted.
108: An admission decision missing authority/rationale or unsupported in its requested
109: scope fails `ADMISSION_INVALID`. Failed admission, selection or update creates
110: no domain records and leaves selection, admitted attachment and parent hashes
111: unchanged; only submitted bytes and the rejection receipt may be stored.
112: Request-identity retries retain the existing idempotence/conflict rules.
113: These are specified postconditions; persistence atomicity remains unimplemented.
114: 
115: ## 4. Chaining and correction examples
116: 
117: UC02 has P=(1/2,1/3,1/6) and P(D=1|s)=(1/2,1,0). Its actual saved payload gives
118: Z_D=7/12 and Q_D=(3/7,4/7,0). The mapping copies that payload into a repaired
119: bundle, then explicitly admits and selects it. The next request refers to that
120: bundle, not to the separately authored UC.posterior fixture.
121: 
122: Specify binary E with P(E=1|D=1,s)=(1,1/2,1) and
123: P(E=1|D=0,s)=(1/4,1/4,1/4). Complements define the other outcomes. This kernel,
124: together with P(D=1|s), defines a coherent joint distribution of (D,E) for every
125: s. The E|D=1 value at c is a stipulated kernel value on a zero-probability D
126: branch; it does not restore c's posterior mass. No conditional independence is
127: assumed. The E update adds only measurement.E, retaining D as consumed and as a
128: conditional argument. The unnormalized masses are (3/7,2/7,0), Z_E|D=5/7 and
129: Q_D,E=(3/5,2/5,0). Direct joint weighting gives (1/4,1/6,0), Z_D,E=5/12 and the
130: same answer. Reusing D as an incremental observation rejects, even under a new
131: request ID. E's distinct atom alone would not suffice without the declared kernel.
132: 
133: UC01's condition A={a,b} yields (3/5,2/5,0) and P(B|A)=2/5 for B={b,c}.
134: After admission, repeating A uses a newly bound event record with the original
135: semantic condition identity and the child's exact view/law. Its denominator is
136: one and masses are unchanged. The second request is a new derivation receipt;
137: mathematical idempotence does not equate record identities. The same semantic
138: condition is stored once. A repeated request ID is separately receipt-idempotent.
139: The original event cannot be silently rebound. Equal member sets alone do not
140: prove equal semantic conditions across changed coordinates or evidence.
141: 
142: UC08's corrected likelihood (1,1/2,1) must apply to the unaffected original
143: prior, giving Z=5/6 and (3/5,1/5,1/5). The repaired mapping names the actual UC02
144: child as superseded and creates an explicit revised evidence/time/admission view.
145: The corrected block supersedes D's reading while retaining semantic atom D. It
146: replaces D once; it is not an extra independent observation. The old posterior
147: and its admission stay readable in their frozen scope, and their current-reuse
148: status is superseded in the new evidence view. The mapping's replay list is
149: explicitly empty. If the D,E descendant is present, it is invalid for current
150: reuse until the conditional E model is revalidated under corrected D and replayed;
151: this example does not silently reuse it. Correction can restore c because its
152: unaffected prior mass is positive; an ordinary update of Q_D cannot do so.
153: 
154: ## 5. Representation alternatives and review boundary
155: 
156: The explicit bundle materializes masses and lets the next request inspect its
157: exact law. A lazy parent-plus-transformation law can avoid duplication, but must
158: have its own immutable law identity, child binding, complete dependency closure,
159: normalizer and consumed information. Its evaluator must resolve to exactly the
160: same measure and reject missing/stale inputs. A provenance pointer to P alone
161: is neither alternative. The fixtures compare both mathematical evaluations on
162: identical inputs, without performance or storage claims. Explicit materialization
163: is provisional here because it makes the boundary reviewable, not because it
164: has been shown superior. General lazy evaluators remain unspecified.
165: 
166: The mapping table and exact chains are proposed examples. Internal calculations
167: and structural checks cannot establish software behavior, accounting validity,
168: empirical forecasting, architecture superiority or human acceptance. Independent
169: review must inspect this supplement with the retained uncertainty/admission/
170: attachment contracts; it must not treat the original child references as repaired
171: in place. P17 still owns continuous/mixed inference, causal methods and calibration.
172: 
173: For a correction, the request's ordinary view/law/attachment/admission fields
174: bind the frozen mathematical input. The separate `target_time_ref`,
175: `target_evidence_view_ref` and `target_admission_ref` authorize the revised inputs
176: and output context. Validate each side against its own context; do not demand
177: that a newly corrected observation was admitted in the frozen base. All output
178: view/law/candidate records bind the target context. A submission of the corrected
179: block as an ordinary incremental update may supply this target authorization;
180: it still fails `CORRECTION_REQUIRES_REBASE` because D has already been consumed.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/uncertainty-rejection-status-contract-v1.md
SHA256: cc5ec2bd69cf4ba0bf4f0d7fb6e6d668cf7975244afa4464fbb7e412c4eeee8c
1: # Rejected uncertainty requests — status correction v1
2: 
3: This supplement corrects five specified rejection receipts in
4: `uncertainty-chaining-fixtures-v1.json`. The independent uncertainty review in
5: `data/accounting-master/runs/20261005T120206-bdb3a74d/P16/execution-0/review_result-0.response.json`
6: returned REVISE: its arithmetic agreed, but the receipts assigned `not_computed`
7: to `mathematical_status`, contrary to uncertainty-contract-v1.md §2. The current
8: fixtures are `uncertainty-chaining-fixtures-v2.json`. The original files and
9: records remain evidence of the defect, not active expected rejection outputs.
10: This proposal correction carries MC08–MC14, MC18, MC25, MC27 and MC33–MC35,
11: principally G1/G6/G15/G16. It requires independent result review.
12: 
13: ## 1. Bounded question and acceptance
14: 
15: Can a consumer interpret all five rejected requests using the existing common
16: status vocabulary? The comparator is uncertainty-contract-v1.md §2 and the
17: unchanged chaining v1 requests and rejection consequences. Acceptance of this
18: slice requires all seven status fields below, exactly five explicit correction
19: mappings, unambiguous active expected receipts, and preservation of every original
20: registry record and every successful case. Changed requests, laws, payloads,
21: masses, normalizers, selected state, parent hashes, audit writes or rejection
22: reasons veto the correction. Missing reference closure or an active malformed
23: predecessor also veto it. These are repair triggers; they do not reject the wider
24: uncertainty research direction. Checker totals explain structural coverage only.
25: 
26: Skeptical audit: the baseline is the declared exchange, not prototype behavior;
27: no arithmetic reassessment, runtime experiment, statistical ranking or environment
28: comparison is needed. The same requests and consequences are compared. Stop after
29: the supplement, versioned fixture, focused preservation report, registered check
30: and updated P16 records. No accounting design begins in this slice.
31: 
32: The status interpretation is a proposed clarification, with provenance in the
33: reviewed repair plan and the governing vocabulary. Its risk is that `unresolved`
34: could be mistaken for permission to continue an invalid request. The decisive
35: `REQUEST_INVALID` disposition, unchanged reason code, explicit no-computation
36: status and no-output postconditions prevent that inference. A focused field and
37: state check is the early diagnostic; independent review decides semantic adequacy.
38: No new empirical, accounting or mathematical default is selected.
39: 
40: ## 2. Common status envelope for pre-evaluation rejection
41: 
42: Request validation decides whether evaluation may proceed. Here it fails before
43: mathematical evaluation, so each corrected receipt contains:
44: 
45: | Field | Value | Meaning for this request |
46: | --- | --- | --- |
47: | `structural_validity` | `invalid` | Request validation failed, including semantic binding/admission/reuse checks. This does not mean the receipt itself is malformed. |
48: | `mathematical_status` | `unresolved` | The requested calculation was not evaluated. This is no finding that its mathematics is false or undefined. |
49: | `computation_status` | `not_computed` | Validation stopped evaluation; there is no computed result. |
50: | `empirical_justification` | `not_established` | The rejection establishes no empirical support. |
51: | `capability_status` | `specified_only_no_runtime` | This file specifies behavior; it reports no runtime execution. |
52: | `accounting_validity` | `not_assessed` | Rejection supplies no accounting eligibility judgment. |
53: | `human_acceptance` | `pending` | Neither fixture consistency nor the prior review establishes human acceptance. |
54: 
55: `disposition="REQUEST_INVALID"` and the existing `reason_codes` remain decisive.
56: `unresolved` never authorizes use of a result or admission of the request. Reserve
57: `undefined` for established undefined mathematics, such as the specified zero
58: normalizer; reserve `failed` for an established mathematical failure. Neither
59: finding was made for these five invalid requests.
60: 
61: The common envelope was chosen over a separate rejection schema because it uses
62: the vocabulary already required of consumers and retains one interpretation for
63: each status field. A separate schema could omit inapplicable mathematical fields,
64: but would require a new discriminator, consumer branches and explicit migration
65: rules. That broader change is unnecessary for this defect. This choice does not
66: settle the later language, interface or persistence design.
67: 
68: ## 3. Versioned correction and active selection
69: 
70: The v2 fixture copies all 125 v1 registry records without canonical JSON changes.
71: It adds exactly five records with the same logical receipt IDs, `version=2`, and
72: the corresponding `@1` receipt as `predecessor`. The only record changes are
73: version, predecessor, the corrected/added status fields, correction provenance,
74: and dependency closure including that predecessor. Requests remain version 1.
75: 
76: | Active case | Corrected receipt | Preserved rejection reason |
77: | --- | --- | --- |
78: | `candidate_only` | `CH.rejection.candidate_only@2` | `ADMISSION_REQUIRED` |
79: | `duplicate_D` | `CH.rejection.duplicate_D@2` | `DUPLICATE_INFORMATION` |
80: | `stale_law` | `CH.rejection.stale_law@2` | `LAW_VIEW_ATTACHMENT_MISMATCH` |
81: | `stale_attachment` | `CH.rejection.stale_attachment@2` | `LAW_VIEW_ATTACHMENT_MISMATCH` |
82: | `correction_on_posterior` | `CH.rejection.correction_on_posterior@2` | `CORRECTION_REQUIRES_REBASE` |
83: 
84: `rejection_status_corrections` is the explicit old-to-new map;
85: `active_rejection_receipts` and the five `cases[].expected_ref` values agree with
86: it. The `historical_rejection_receipts` entries mark each predecessor ineligible
87: as an active expected output. Registry presence alone confers no eligibility.
88: Consumers of this proposal select the named v2 fixture and its explicit case
89: references; they must not infer a version by scanning for the latest ID. Successful
90: case references, bundles, admissions and representation alternatives are unchanged.
91: 
92: This is a revision of authored specification examples, not a runtime replay or
93: an exception to request idempotency. No runtime receipt has been overwritten or
94: reissued. A future runtime implementing a fixed specification must still return
95: the same logical receipt for identical request bytes; operational migration and
96: historical replay rules remain obligations of the unfinished persistence contract.
97: 
98: ## 4. Unchanged rejection consequences and retained evidence
99: 
100: Every corrected receipt retains its request reference, exact reasons, selected
101: family/view/attachment/model snapshots, selection revision and parent hashes.
102: `state_before == state_after`, `domain_writes=[]`, the audit writes remain
103: `submitted_bytes` and `rejection_receipt`, and both `proposed_view_ref` and the
104: output `model_ref` remain null. No posterior, aggregate result or candidate view
105: is created. Corrected provenance identifies the pinned predecessor file and
106: terminal review; it is not a new observation or likelihood factor.
107: 
108: The current focused diagnostic is preserved in `uncertainty-rejection-status-check-v2.json`
109: with its command and input hashes; the fresh registered result and execution
110: record use `uncertainty-rejection-status-structural-check-v1.json` and
111: `uncertainty-rejection-status-structural-execution-v1.json`. Their actual outcomes
112: are recorded in P16/result.md. They test specification consistency, not runtime
113: enforcement. Five counterpart contracts, the integrated initial profile and
114: dependency order, fourteen open decisions, P17 methods, E05 and all wider mission
115: obligations remain. Accounting validity, empirical forecast validity, product
116: readiness and human acceptance remain unestablished.
117: 
118: Diagnostic correction: the first check treated the frozen `perimeter` value
119: `single_entity@1` as a registry reference solely because it contained `@1`.
120: It is a scope label in these fixtures; the v2 diagnostic excludes that field
121: from registry-reference traversal. The failed v1 report and checker source are
122: preserved. This changes no request or specified receipt. General perimeter
123: representation remains outside this bounded repair.


FILE: docs/design/proposal/architecture/architecture-integration-v1.md
SHA256: 29fbdc2f42ab45aff4829994db85592228b13907d2ad8676fe5ec5fc32eecfb1
1: # Integration of the nine proposed contracts
2: 
3: The active contract list in P16 architecture.json is the document index. This
4: supplement resolves interactions among those contracts without altering earlier
5: evidence or treating saved fixture outcomes as executed product behavior. It
6: addresses MC02–MC14, MC18–MC27 and MC33–MC35. The baseline is the five existing
7: P16 contracts and their accepted amendments; Core 0/P03/P07 are investigated
8: prototypes, not automatic defaults.
9: 
10: ## Meaning carried between contracts
11: 
12: | Boundary | Meaning that must survive | Rejection or unresolved consequence |
13: | --- | --- | --- |
14: | Evidence → time | Original bytes, source occurrence, review layer, economic/reporting/publication/ingestion clocks | Missing clocks cannot support stronger historical-knowledge claims |
15: | Evidence/time → history | Scoped admitted interpretation/assumption, joint symbols, conflicts and exclusions | Faithful reading alone cannot become an admitted accounting constraint |
16: | History → uncertainty | Full family/view, partition, dependence and query-relevant economic memory | No weights from witness counts; missing law gives a symbolic/unweighted result |
17: | Scenario/history → accounting | Branch opening, exact event identity, date/context, permitted information and eligible method | Unresolved or unsupported treatment produces no posting |
18: | Accounting → persistence | Validated request, source/method versions, proposal/delta and expected head | Exact replay before freshness; all-or-none transition; unknown commit reconciled under original identity |
19: | Calculation → report | Common history/scenario index, native statuses, unresolved regions/mass and unit/time semantics | No successful-subset renormalization or independent selection of line extrema |
20: | Correction → all descendants | Immutable old view, replacement scope, candidate-set membership and transitive dependency | Current reuse invalidated even for descendants created after the correction |
21: | Any contract → IR/interface | Versioned type, lineage and admission requirements | Unknown schema or required semantics cannot be ignored or coerced |
22: 
23: ## Explicit amendments and precedence
24: 
25: L1's portable encoding applies only to new portable records. Existing native
26: record digests, original files, source byte hashes and receipts stay unchanged.
27: Integer metadata remains integer; financial quantities use exact rationals.
28: There is no global NFC normalization. A named legacy import retains both IDs
29: and checked decoded correspondence. Source-map identity is separate from model
30: identity, so formatting changes do not alter a model's financial meaning.
31: 
32: P2 resolves the early accounting contract's ambiguous “event/version” language:
33: the stable lookup key excludes version and payload. A correction uses a new
34: business-event identity with an explicit treatment/lineage; merely changing a
35: version under an existing key conflicts. Branch identity includes an explicit
36: projection namespace, distinguishing separately requested hypotheticals from
37: retries of the same projection. No double counting of hypothetical projections
38: is permitted when reporting realized transactions.
39: 
40: Native accounting retains its established validation order and assess-only
41: boundary. New portable wrappers validate their own envelopes and then delegate
42: to that native order. Dependency recognition is guarded by reference shape;
43: malformed refs are not followed. A shape error cannot be masked by finding an
44: old receipt. Output defects are distinguished from ineligible economic events:
